Summary

When Derek Bishop died, he passed his debts with Kingpin down to his unknowing wife to bear. When Eleanor Bishop was lost in the snap, the debt fell to her oblivious daughter. Freshly orphaned sixteen-year-old Kate Bishop owed Kingpin a debt that no amount of money would repay.

She would have to work for it.

you can pay what you want but you're never gonna own me

by Charlie_Balle

    Chapter 1

    prologue Perhaps there was some sort of cruel irony that half the world disappeared when Kate was mid- argument with her mother.

    Half of the world was gone in a second. Kate stood in the hallway, trying to connect the pieces in her brain as she tried not to breathe in any of the dust that had gotten on her clothes. As she tried not to breathe in her mother.

    Where had her mother gone? Surely the dust sitting in a pile on the floor where her mother stood moments ago wasn’t her. It couldn’t be. People just didn’t turn to ash.

    But Kate Bishop could hear screaming and wailing outside, people calling out for loved ones, and somewhere inside of her, she knew.

    She couldn’t even remember what the argument she had been screaming moments earlier had been about. Some petty thing that Kate did because she was feeling particularly spiteful she was sure.

    That was how they worked. Kate did something stupid and her mother lectured her and tensions were gone for the night after Kate apologized. Kate couldn’t even remember how it started. Perhaps her father’s death was what drove a wedge between her and her mother. The only way Kate could guarantee her mother’s full attention was when she acted out.

    But Kate loved her mom, and standing there in the hallway as the dust clung to her clothes like one last attempt at a hug, Kate suddenly felt very alone.

    She suddenly felt like she was standing there in the foyer looking through the hole in the wall as the Avengers fought aliens as a kid and she had no idea where her parents were. The same feeling washed over her and Kate’s stomach violently recoiled.

    Kate couldn’t remember the rest of the day. She somehow ended up from the hallway to having showered and changed, standing in the doorway to her mother’s bedroom where the sheets were still unmade after her mother had to make a quick leave that morning. Nothing felt real. She wanted it to be some horrible nightmare that she desperately wanted to wake up from.

    Kate felt like a small child as she moved into the bedroom and crawled into her mother’s bed. Cocooned in the blanket, Kate finally felt like she was able to pull out her phone and check the news. She could barely make it past the first few articles that confirmed she wasn’t dreaming before she threw her phone across the room and rolled over to bundle herself up in the blanket more.

    Kate couldn’t sleep. The longer she lay in the soft bed the longer she realized that things were suddenly going to go to shit for her if they found out her mother was gone. Bishop Security was going to collapse without her mother there to lead it.

    Kate suddenly wished that she had paid attention to all those times her mother lectured her about learning the ropes so that she could have a steady job when she graduated. She reached up to dig the heels of her palms into her eyes and clenched her jaw to stamp down the wave of emotion that rolled through her.

    She couldn’t let herself have a meltdown, not when there was so much work to do. She just had to fake it until she made it. Bishop Security wouldn’t listen to her at her age but if she could just pretend to be her mother until things were okay then Kate would be able to prove to her mother that she could handle responsibility.

    The reminder of the empty house just made Kate’s eyes water and she sucked in a sharp breath, soft sobs catching in her throat.

    The Avengers had to do something. They saved the world from aliens, they could bring the people back too. Kate knew it. If there was anybody who could do it then it was them.

    Kate eventually dragged herself out of bed, wrapping the blanket around her shoulders like a cape as she made her way to her mother’s office and sank into the chair, staring at the computer before she used her mother’s credentials to log in.

    Kate left a trace of who people would assume was Eleanor Bishop in the company. Enough to keep it from collapsing. Enough to keep people from being suspicious. Enough to keep people from trying to ‘adopt’ the newly orphaned Bishop kid. Enough to keep herself safe.

    Kate then logged out and her feet found their way upstairs to her bedroom. The sight of the Hawkeye poster she had pinned to the wall suddenly made her feel sick. The news reported that not a single peep had been heard from them. Some part of Kate wondered if they were gone too. If they were gone then nobody was left to save the world. Nobody was left to give Kate her mother back. Nobody was there to help her figure out what the fuck she was supposed to do.

    Kate sat on the bed in her room, still wrapped up in the sheet from her mother’s bed as she stared at the Hawkeye poster on her wall.

    All she could think about was that she hadn’t apologized to her mother. They were supposed to fight and make up.

    Never before had they just fought.

    The next few days were a big smear to Kate. She couldn’t recall eating or sleeping, just sitting for hours in the chair in her mother’s office and doing her best not to sound stupid as she poked at things with Bishop Security and impersonated her mother.

    She held the blanket around her shoulders like a shield from the outside world. She watched television in small increments to catch up on the news, unable to stomach the clips of walls where people posted photos of missing loved ones, trying desperately to see who was gone and who wasn’t.

    The news coined the term “The Decimation” to describe what had happened. People were out of control, rioting, and looting, and screaming for loved ones. Kate could only stomach minutes of it at a time.

    The fifth day after Kate Bishop watched her mother turn to dust in front of her eyes, there was a knock on the door that had her fearing that she had been caught. She carefully shuffled to the door, a baseball bat clutched in her hand just because she wasn’t sure if it was looters.

    She opened the door and found that she suddenly had to crane her head up to peer at the tall figure looming over her, her grasp on the bat wavering.

    “You must be Kate Bishop,” the man said, standing in the doorway. Kate's heart beat hard against her ribcage.

    “Wilson Fisk…” Kate breathed out in recognition, unsure whether she wanted to slam the door in his face or run away.

    “Please, call me Kingpin,” he smiled at her, tearing his eyes away to peer above her into the apartment. “Is your mother home?”

    Kate felt the words clog in her throat and she stood in the doorway like a fool as she clutched the blanket around her shoulders with one hand, a bat in the other. “She’s not available right now.”

    Kingpin’s eyes flicked from whatever he was looking at to land on her and Kate suddenly felt very small.

    “Ah.” A knowing look crossed his face. “I see. My condolences for your loss.”

    Kate lost her grip on the bat and it clattered to the floor. She remembered everyone apologizing for the loss of her father as a child but her mother wasn’t dead. She was just… gone. But she would come back. The Avengers would bring her back. They had to.

    “May I come in?” Kingpin asked. “We have a lot to discuss.”

    Kate obediently stepped aside because while she had never listened very much to her mother, she had been warned of the dangers of the world and she knew that inviting a very dangerous man into her home was one of the things that would have earned her a very large lecture.

    But her mother wasn’t there. Kate had to pull herself together and be a big girl because some whiny sixteen-year-old rich brat wasn’t going to win in whatever game Kingpin was going to play.

    “Come,” Kingpin’s hand clasped onto her shoulder to lead her further into the house with a familiarity that frightened Kate. “I have been waiting so long to meet you, Kate Bishop.” debt Chapter Summary The fact that Kingpin came alone told Kate a lot and nothing all at once.

    The fact that Kingpin came alone told Kate a lot and nothing all at once.

    He had come expecting her mother. He had been wanting to meet her. He had not anticipated a fight. He hadn’t needed anyone to come with him.

    Kate shed the blanket somewhere between the foyer and the kitchen. The fabric she used as a shield against the outside world would not protect her from her situation and only served to make her look like a child.

    “Please, sit,” Kingpin said as if he were in his own home and Kate had burst in unannounced. Still, Kate does sit at the table, watching as he sank into the seat at the head of the table where her father used to sit.

    For a moment, there was silence, and Kate stared at the large man in front of her, waiting for him to say something-- anything.

    “How old are you now, Kate?” He asked, her name falling from his mouth with a familiarity that made Kate’s skin crawl. She had a feeling he knew the answer. She told him anyway.

    “Sixteen.” Kate tried her best to keep her tone from wavering, her chest still tight.

    “Ah,” he hummed, leaning back in the chair with ease as his eyes raked over her. “You have grown into a little lady then.”

    Kate bristled slightly at the condescension in his voice, taking a deep breath before she misspoke. “I’m afraid that I don’t understand why you’re here.”

    “Ah, straight down to business,” Kingpin chuckled. “You’re just like your mother.”

    The reminder of her mom made Kate feel like she was punched in the gut all over again and suddenly she couldn’t help but wonder if some of the dust was still clinging to her because no matter how many times she showered, she still felt like there was something on her skin.

    “Very well.” Kingpin straightened slightly and Kate roughly yanked herself back together. “You owe me a debt, Kate Bishop.”

    “I’ve never even met you before,” Kate retorted before she could think twice.

    Kingpin laughed. “But I’ve known about you since you were nine.”

    Something curdled in Kate’s stomach, the thought of such a dangerous man watching her when she still had baby teeth. She frantically tried to think of some reason she would have posed interest to him.

    “Your father cared about you very much,” he continued and Kate forgot how to breathe for a few moments at the casual mention of a man long past gone beneath the dirt. “He came to me and begged me for help. He refused to let his little Kate suffer. And because I am a nice man, I helped him out.”

    Kate felt like she should realize the weight of the situation but there was static ringing in her ears and she desperately wished she didn’t leave the blanket in the hallway.

    “Your poor mother was so confused at all the debt he owed. We have to collect it, it’s not fair for us to be put out over greediness.” Kingpin laced his fingers together on the table, the picture of a calm and collected man. “She worked hard for you.”

    Kate suddenly thought about every missed tournament, late-night returns, missed dinners, and how she would scream at her mom for never being there for her. Guilt pooled in her stomach where it hardened uncomfortably like a rock.

    “Your mother is gone now, may she rest in peace,” Kingpin sounded solemn, like he was truly saddened to hear that Kate’s mom was gone.

    Kate swallowed down the urge to tell him that her mom wasn’t gone forever and that the Avengers were going to fix things. She didn’t want to be laughed at like a child.

    “That brings me to the matter at hand,” Kingpin shook off the heavy pressure of the atmosphere and the weight of his eyes fell onto Kate. “The Bishop’s still owe me a debt.”

    Kate tried to remember how to make her tongue work. “How much money?” She had no idea how to negotiate or why her mom hadn’t paid him off or why they were living in luxury if they were in so much debt.

    Kingpin’s laugh was so loud that it made Kate flinch. “Silly girl,” he tutted. “This is not a kind of debt you can pay with money.”

    “Then what kind of debt it is?” Perhaps, Kate mused, if she listened to her mom then she would understand better what was being unsaid.

    When Kingpin grinned, he used too many teeth, much like Kate would think a shark would smile if it could. It made her think of Bruce from the movie Finding Nemo, one that Kate had watched with her mom during one of their nights after an explosive fight that turned into a tentative truce where Kate could just have her mom. “I think we can help each other here.”

    “I don’t want more debt,” Kate quickly said because she literally couldn’t afford whatever kind of debt her father apparently had.

    Kingpin shook his head. “Think of it more as our interests aligning.”

    “The debt,” Kate tried to steer the conversation back on track before she agreed to something she shouldn’t. “What kind?”

    “You have your father’s temper,” he replied. Kate felt like he was taking advantage of the sore spot left behind as he casually flung around mentions of her parents and the secrets they had kept from her. “You’re of no use to me if someone finds out about your little… ruse.”

    Kate suddenly thought about the hours she spent in the plush chair in her mom’s office, tapping away at the keyboard in an attempt to convince the world that Eleanor Bishop was still alive. The fact that Kingpin not only knew but could hold it over her head told Kate that she wouldn’t walk out of the conversation winning. “So?”

    “Tell me, Kate,” Kingpin started, the use of her name still too intimate. “How long have you been cultivating your skills in defense?”

    Kate blinked. “What?”

    “The day of your father’s funeral, was it not?” Kingpin continued on. “Seven years you’ve been training to protect yourself and your mother. Has it come in handy?”

    Kate suddenly recalled cutting through Central Park late at night when she was fourteen, the way the hand grabbed her shoulder, the way the man whispered to her that he wasn’t going to hurt her, the way she sunk her teeth into his arm and threw him on the concrete and ensured that he didn’t get back up.

    She thought of the way she came home and the scolding on the tip of her mom’s tongue died the moment she saw her. She thought of the way she sobbed into her mom’s arms as she rocked her and the way her mom whispered that she was safe. She thought of the way she hadn’t realized there was blood on her chin and in her teeth until her mom pressed a glass of lukewarm water into her hands and told her to swish her mouth out and spit like Kate was little and had lost her first tooth.

    Kate stared at the table, her heart thudding uncomfortably against her ribcage as the man in front of her waited for an answer. She couldn’t find the words stuck inside of her so she merely nodded, digging her fingers into the lounge pants she wore as she did her best to ignore the phantom metallic taste in her mouth.

    “His name was Ben Henderson,” Kingpin said as if he knew the exact moment she was thinking of. Kate held her breath, staring so hard at the table that she could see each little imperfection. “Your mother asked me to take care of him.”

    Kate tried to think of two years ago, her mother calling up a dangerous man to ask him to handle someone who hurt her daughter, another debt to add to the pile. “Thanks…”

    Kingpin gave an amused grunt at her loss for words. “I am not a bad man, Kate. I’ve helped your father, I’ve helped your mother, and now I’m going to help you.”

    “And if I say no?” Kate dared to ask, the threat lost on the way that she couldn’t look him in the eye. She regretted her words the moment they left her lips.

    “I think we both know what happens then.” Kingpin leaned forward, his demeanor settling into something serious. “You are going to work for me now, Kate. And in return, I am going to give you the same protection I gave your parents.”

    “I’m sixteen,” Kate said as if her words were a shield that could protect her from the situation settling on her shoulders. “I’m still in school.”

    “You won’t have to worry about school anymore,” Kingpin shook his head, and the fact that he wasn’t concerned about her age made Kate’s insides twist. “I will teach you all you need to know.”

    Kingpin then stood as if their conversation was finished and part of Kate wanted to demand more answers from him. Another part of her told her that she would get nowhere so she moved to stand as well.

    “No need,” Kingpin assured her. “I can let myself out.”

    Kate heard the front door shut moments later. Part of her felt like pinching herself to see if it was all a dream. Then her eyes fell onto a tiny white business card that had been left behind on the table that held nothing but a phone number and suddenly it all felt real.

    The first thing she did was lock the door before she finally let herself fall apart. She screamed and chucked an expensive vase across the room and watched as it shattered against the wall before she kicked at furniture and walls until she was left violently sobbing like a small child.

    It wasn’t fair. The whole thing wasn’t fair. Her parents lied to her. She didn’t even know. They left everything behind for her to handle. She hated them.

    She loved them.

    She wasn’t sure what she felt anymore other than suffocating loneliness. She wanted her mom to just tell her what to do and how to make things better. She wanted to tell her mom that she forgave her for her absences and that she was never really angry.

    She would promise to be the perfect daughter if her mom just came back. She wouldn’t get into fights, she wouldn’t cause trouble, she wouldn’t make a peep.

    She eventually calmed down and took in the sight of her destruction, wiping away tears from her face as she told herself to get it together because she wasn’t a baby.

    She then picked herself back up, cleaned up the broken shards of vase, realized she made her foot bleed with her kicks, cleaned herself up, and then stood in the kitchen once again as she stared at the business card on the table.

    She finally picked it up to run her thumb along the ink stamped into the paper, recognizing how expensive it was. Her fingers felt something on the back and she turned the card over.

    KATHERINE ELIZABETH BISHOP

    Her full name, the one on her birth certificate that no one but her mother used when she was really angry, was stamped on the back in a tiny font. It wasn’t written, it was permanently penned there with ink like the number on the other side.

    Kingpin hadn’t known her mother was gone when he had shown up at her door so Kate tried to think of why he would have a card with her name on it ready on hand.

    A debt not paid with money, a promise of protection, and a lingering sense of dread were all that Kate was left with.

    She swished her mouth out with lukewarm water to get rid of the phantom taste of blood in her mouth, watching it swirl down the drain with not a tinge of red in it, and Kate couldn’t stop herself from biting down on her tongue harder and harder until a burst of iron filled her mouth and dripped into the sink in a deep crimson color.

    Kate gagged as she thought of the man- Ben Henderson, she hadn’t known his name before- and the way his blood had once filled her mouth. She thought of the way her mom held her and Kate could pretend for just a moment that her mom was right there rubbing her back as she swished her mouth out again and spat pink water into the sink.

    When she went to bed that night, she caught sight of the poster on her wall and her childish hope that the Avengers would fix everything. Kate knew she was naive but she didn't care anymore.

    She took down the Hawkeye poster on her bedroom wall.

    She told herself she’d put it back up when her mom returned.

    zip ties Chapter Summary Kate sees her own face in a list of those unconfirmed alive.

    Kate didn’t sleep that night. She hadn’t even tried because she knew that it would end up going nowhere. She laid in bed for hours before finally giving in and getting up in the wee morning hours to shuffle downstairs.

    Her mom’s office chair was starting to feel more like her bed from how often she spent in it. She sat down in it, turned to face the computer, and watched the screensaver sift through photographs of different places worldwide.

    Kate wouldn’t be so naive to think that Kingpin wasn’t watching her moves. At least he probably was. She wasn’t positive. She also wasn’t sure if she wanted to know.

    She sighed, checked the news, and listened as they talked about people buying out empty plots for loved ones, suicide rates driven up at the loss of family, and a casket crisis as funeral parlors were run ragged. There was a new segment on the news where they scrolled through photos of people trying to find out if loved ones not living near them were lost in The Decimation.

    Kate shut it off when a picture of her classmate came on the screen, her parents trying to find out if she was alive after sending her to live with her cousin because she was troubled.

    It wasn’t until Kate realized that phone lines were so busy that calls and texts were getting delayed that she realized friends from school thought she was lost as well. Kate had been so busy ensuring that the public knew about Eleanor Bishop being alive that she had done nothing to assure them Kate Bishop was.

    Kate had been ready to reply to her friends, a text already typed up and ready to go when she realized that perhaps it was better that the world thought she was dead.

    Eleanor Bishop refusing to leave her home, mourning the loss of her only child, refusing to be seen in public, shut in and away just like after she lost her husband.

    Kate decided to not announce her continued existence to anyone but also not deter suspicion.

    Four weeks after The Decimation, schools attempted to start back up. Kate didn’t go, not that she hadn’t wanted to but because Kignpin’s words rang in her head. She hadn’t heard from him in a month and that made her antsy.

    But she couldn’t put her life on hold waiting for something so she finally had to push herself back into the world because she ran out of food. Lots of restaurants and take-out places had shut down so Kate couldn’t exactly order in. She had to put on clothes like a proper person for once and step out into the world.

    The sun hurt her eyes and the streets were eerily quiet. Kate pulled the hood of her sweatshirt over her head to cover her face because it had been so long since she had actually seen the sun. She hadn’t realized that she had locked herself away.

    She passed by the road that led to her school on the way to the store and feet automatically took her to the front step. The chain link fence around the school had yearbook photos zip-tied to them of students who had yet to be confirmed alive on the left side and those who were confirmed gone on the right.

    Kate stared at her own photo amongst the sea of students she had gone to school with who the world didn't know if they were dead or alive. She almost felt like she was looking at a stranger, her own face grinning into the camera even though her mom told her to “take a nice picture” because Kate wasn’t just going to sit there and smile pretty like she was at a charity auction, she wanted to leave her mark.

    She couldn’t bring herself to look at the other side of the fence where the students who were gone were. Some part of her didn’t want to know. She turned on her foot and went to the store, realizing halfway through grabbing necessities that she was picking up enough for two.

    It wasn’t like she went shopping for groceries often. Either her mom did the shopping or she had someone do it for them. Kate hadn’t learned how much a gallon of milk was until she was twelve. It wasn’t until she was on an independence streak with a group of “bad” kids from her school that she learned more about how to shop and pick things out for herself.

    In the end, Kate ended up getting bread, peanut butter, jelly, and microwave meals that her mom always said were stuffed with grease. She didn’t need milk or eggs because she wasn’t going to cook anything. She knew how to but she simply didn’t have the energy.

    Kate was on her way home when she felt the way someone was following just behind her. She tried not to panic, the phantom taste of iron filling her mouth as she tried to keep her calm and twisted her fingers around the handles of her grocery bags to use them as a weapon if she had to.

    “There’s no need for hostility,” a man spoke right behind her. “I’m here on behalf of Wilson Fisk.”

    Kate spun around, ready to defend herself, only to find herself looking at a rather non-threatening- looking man. He gave her a reassuring smile as if he was trying to convince her that he meant no harm.

    “He’s in a car just around the corner and waiting for you,” he continued.

    “How do I know you’re telling the truth?” Kate demanded because she wasn’t approaching some random car to be kidnapped because she was stupid.

    The man reached into his pocket and Kate tensed up. He saw her reaction and paused before he slowly pulled a card out of his pocket and held it out.

    It was another blank white business card with a phone number embossed on the front. He turned the card in his fingers to reveal her full name and Kate sighed. “Yeah, okay. Show me.”

    The car was rather expensive and Kate swallowed hard as she approached, feeling foolish as she gripped her bags of shopping. Rather than roll down the window like in some cheesy mafia movie, the man reached out and opened the door for her, gesturing for her to enter.

    Kate peered in and locked eyes with Kingpin. She knew she had no other choice and slowly entered the vehicle, setting her bags on the floor as she did her best not to flinch when the car door shut harshly and locked.

    “Apologies for not reaching out sooner, Kate,” Kingpin said as the car started to move. “I have been rather busy trying to smooth things over in wake of the… what was the term they coined?”

    It took Kate a few moments to remember how her mouth worked. “The Decimation.”

    “Right,” Kingpin nodded. “The Decimation. Quite a harsh word, don't you think? Do you know what a decimation is, Kate?”

    “Uh,” Kate took a deep breath because it was an easy question. “The killing or destruction of a large portion of a group or species.”

    “There is another definition,” Kingpin said, looking out the window as they drove by Kate’s school which was out of the way of Kate’s usual path. “It was also a form of Roman military discipline where every tenth man in a set was executed by members of his cohort as a punishment for the whole group.”

    Kate wasn’t sure what to do with the information he was telling her. She wasn’t sure how to reply. He seemed to find her silence amusing.

    He then reached into his pocket and Kate couldn’t help but tense up, confused when he pulled out a zip tie. For a moment, cold fear surged through Kate as she thought about how he could bind her wrists together but instead, he held it out to her to take.

    Kate took it warily and he looked out the window again. Kate realized that they had stopped in front of her school.

    “I want you to go out there and take your photo down from one side and zip it to the other,” Kingpin told her. “Return to me when you’ve finished, I’ll be waiting right here.”

    It took Kate a moment to comprehend his order. He wanted her to declare herself dead. Kate stared at the photographs flapping in the wind

    “Kate,” he warned impatiently and Kate reached out, finding that the door had been unlocked and she was let out. She thought briefly about running as she walked up to the fence, ready to abandon her groceries, but she knew deep down that it was a foolish plan and that no matter how far she ran, she would be found.

    Kate found her picture again and reached out to take it, only having to give one harsh tug to rip the top from the current zip tie. It was almost like a missing person flyer, ones that Kate would see in the newspaper at the bottom or imagine on milk cartons from movies.

    Her feet were heavy as she slowly trudged to the other side of the fence and found an empty spot. She zip-tied her photo to the fence and took a step back, unable to help herself from looking as her photo was surrounded by a sea of dead children with smiling faces.

    She caught sight of one of her friends and her breath stuttered, caught in her chest as a fresh wave of grief poured over her. She looked away sharply, taking a few deep breaths to gather herself because the last thing she needed was to return to Kingpin crying.

    She walked back to the car and slid back into the backseat, taking one more deep breath. The car started moving again and Kate peered up at Kingpin.

    “Very good.” He let out a pleased hum that made Kate’s stomach twist. “You know what this means, don’t you?”

    Kate was surprised at how steady she managed to keep her voice as she replied. “I’m dead.”

    “You’re dead,” he nodded solemnly. “The world has no need for Kate Bishop anymore.”

    Kate felt the suffocating quiet as the car descended into silence. She wasn’t sure what to say or if she should even say anything. Kingpin seemed content to peer out the window and wait so Kate took the moment to inspect her surroundings as her jar of peanut butter hit the side of her shoe with each left turn the car took.

    She felt extremely undressed and out of place. She had been in plenty of fancy cars but Kingpin wore a neatly pressed suit while she was dressed in a ratty sweatshirt and jeans.

    Kate couldn’t keep silent any longer when the car drove into a part of the city she was unfamiliar with. “Where are we going?”

    Kingpin turned to look at her, a small grin on his face and Kate realized he had expected her to ask. “Let’s call it school.”

    Kate blinked. “You said I don’t need school. You’d teach me everything.”

    “I’m aware of what I said,” Kingpin said, his voice a tad harsh, and Kate realized she misstepped and pushed too far. “But even I cannot teach you everything.”

    Kate chewed on the inside of her lower lip anxiously, trying to swallow down nausea that was bubbling up from her twisted stomach. She wondered if her mom had ever been taken in a car like she was or if Kingpin was doing it because Kate was sixteen and still in need of ‘school’, whatever that meant.

    They stopped outside of an unmarked shop and the door was opened. Kingpin gestured for Kate to get out and Kate couldn’t help but briefly glance at her groceries.

    “I will ensure they are returned to you,” Kingpin told her and she had to take him at his word because she had no other choice.

    The man holding the door open offered her his hand but Kate rejected it, stepping out on her own. She realized moments later that Kingpin was getting out too and his hand fell on her shoulder, a little too firm to be entirely friendly.

    “This is where you will learn,” Kingpin said as he led her inside the shop. It looked abandoned inside, the shelves half filled and nobody manning the counter. “A car will pick you up Monday, Thursday, and Saturday to bring you here and back. You don't have a license to drive, do you?”

    Kate shook her head as they stopped in front of a door to an employee lounge. Kingpin pushed it open and led her inside. She should have known that the store was a front and that they weren’t going to an actual employee lounge. The room looked like a dojo, one not dissimilar to where she had learned martial arts when she was younger.

    Before Kate could decipher why she was there, Kingpin gestured to a man with tanned skin and dark hair that was cropped short who was in the room. “This is Calvin Bailey. He will be your teacher.”

    Kate could recognize instantly that Calvin was the type of teacher she usually hated in school. The way he held himself reminded her of some of her strict martial arts teachers, ones that would criticize everything that she did and nothing was ever good enough for them.

    “Calvin here will be testing you today,” Kingpin explained before his hand left her shoulder. “When he is finished you will return to the front where a car will be waiting to take you home.”

    Kate almost asked him if he wasn’t going to be staying before biting her tongue. She didn’t want him to stay but at the same time, she didn’t want to be alone with a strange man she didn’t know. She listened as Kingpin left, the door shutting heavily behind him.

    “A sweatshirt and jeans are usually not clothes one would grace a dojo in,” Calvin commented, already critiquing her.

    “Yeah, well, I didn’t know I was coming or I would have dressed better,” Kate snapped before exhaling harshly. “What test am I doing?”

    Calvin tutted. “This is my dojo, you will respect me when you are in it. You will call me Sifu. We are not friends. You are here to learn and I am here to teach. Do we have an understanding?”

    Kate resisted the urge to groan, already frustrated. Still, she learned begrudging respect from her many years of suffering through strict teachers in school so she nodded. “Yes, Sifu,” she grit out.

    “Good,” Calvin sniffed before he nodded to her converse sneakers. “Shoes off and step forward.”

    Kate toed her shoes off and took her socks off as well, already knowing that if she was going to be fighting then socks would put her at a great disadvantage. She stepped forward toward him, desperately wishing that she could just go home.

    “You will fight me in whatever style of martial arts you are most comfortable with,” Calvin told her. “You are not to stop until I tell you. Do you understand?”

    Kate had so many questions but they were all clogged in her throat so she simply gave a curt nod and then immediately had to dodge out of the way as Calvin lunged.

    She recognized immediately that Calvin was not like her teachers or adults she would fight against in her other martial arts classes. They always handled her cautiously, like they were afraid of damaging a rich kid and getting sued.

    Calvin held no such reservations and Kate had to keep dodging his hands as she stumbled around the room.

    “You are like a flighty little birdie,” Calvin told her, his tone informing her that his words were not a compliment. “You cannot run away forever, little birdie. Fight back. We will not finish until you do.”

    Kate was eventually too slow to avoid one of his grabs and was thrown to the ground in an instant, the air knocked from her lungs as she slammed against the not-well-padded floor. Calvin gave her no chance to catch her breath, already on top of her. Kate struggle to try and suck air into her lungs as Calvin was off of her moments afterward.

    “Kneel,” Calvin ordered and Kate tried to do what he said. Calvin grabbed her upper arms when she did, kicking her foot forward and forcing her to kneel on one knee before he grabbed her wrists and yanked her arms above her head. “Arch your back.”

    Kate leaned back to do so and within a few seconds she could breathe again as the spasms stealing her breath stopped. Calvin kept his hold firm around her wrists so Kate waited, taking deep breaths to stop the light-headed feeling.

    “Breathe,” Calvin told her firmly. “Thirty seconds.”

    Exactly thirty seconds later, Calvin let her wrists go and Kate warily pushed herself to her feet to face him.

    “We will begin again and you will fight me properly this time,” Calvin informed her, leaving no room for questions. “Get into position.”

    When they began again, Kate actually attempted to fight back. Calvin knew what he was doing but Kate knew how to escape some of his grabs, only able to gain the upper hand for a moment before Calvin was back in control. Kate ended up flat on her back again but she had time to prepare and fell in a way that wouldn’t knock the air out of her lungs.

    They went again. And again. Calvin kept calling her a little birdie and sharply telling her to fight back even though Kate was. He kept ordering her to get up again and again.

    Kate wasn’t sure how long they went. Her bruises had bruises and her hair was plastered to her face with sweat as her hoodie clung uncomfortably to her sweaty frame.

    “Hmm,” Calvin hummed as Kate panted to catch her breath, not quite as sweaty as Kate. “I see. You are dismissed. I expect you to have an ice bath as soon as you return to your residence and soak for three minutes. I will see you Thursday.”

    Kate stared at him for a few moments before she stepped toward her shoes, trying her best not to whimper in pain as she bent down to pull them on. She didn’t bother tugging socks on her feet nor did she try to tie her shoes. She shuffled out the door and through the abandoned store.

    “Miss Bishop?” A man greeted her when she exited and Kate eyed him. “Your ride is over here.”

    It was a different car with a different driver but Kate was too exhausted to ask questions and slipped in when the man held the door open for her.

    “Would you like the AC on, Miss Bishop?” The driver asked when Kate winced as her damp sweatshirt was pressed against her back.

    “Uh,” Kate looked up at the man. “Yeah, sure.”

    The cold air blowing on her felt like heaven and she watched as the car navigated through the unfamiliar part of the city back toward where her home was. The door was opened once again and when she was offered a hand, Kate took it and let the man help her out of the car.

    “Kingpin has a gift for you,” the man stopped her before she went inside and Kate was filled with dread as he opened the trunk. To her surprise, it was just a large bag of ice that the man hauled out. “I’m, uh, supposed to deliver this right to your bathtub.”

    “I can do it.” Kate was absolutely exhausted but there was no way she was letting an unfamiliar man into her home where she would be alone.

    The man eyed her as if he didn’t believe her but seemed to understand her reservations and passed the heavy bag over. It was uncomfortably cold and heavy, the ice digging into her skin as she hauled it over her shoulder.

    “Thanks for the ride,” Kate mumbled before she arrived home. She had done a few ice baths in the past after hard competitions but she still dreaded it as she carried the bag to her bathroom, dumping the bag of ice into the tub.

    It wasn’t until she sat curled up, teeth chattering in icy water, that she remembered she left her groceries behind. She would have to eat whatever she could scrounge up from the fridge because she was not going out shopping again.

    Kate finished her ice bath, got dressed and wrapped herself in a blanket, and then flopped onto the couch to watch the news and rest her aching joints.

    She ignored her rumbling stomach as she flipped through news channels until a knock on her door had her freezing. She wanted to cry from the stress of the day and desperately hoped that whoever was bothering her was telling her that her house was on fire because she didn’t want to leave again unless it was absolutely necessary.

    When she opened the door, there was nobody there. Kate looked left and then right on the empty street before her eyes looked down. At her feet were bags of groceries, ones that she hadn’t ordered in paper bags filled with fresh produce and meat along with the plastic bags of food she had purchased, the frozen microwave meals already long since thawed and useless.

    Kate hauled the groceries inside and only unpacked what had to be refrigerated immediately, tossing the frozen microwave meals in the trash before slapping some peanut butter on bread and eating that for dinner.

    When she sat in her mom’s office that night, she noticed that the inbox for her mom’s email was filled with dozens of new messages and she only had to open one to realize what they were about.

    Sympathies for Eleanor about the death of her daughter. Someone had seen the picture Kingpin had Kate move and news spread. Kate deleted email after email expressing condolences for her own death, unable to help herself from reading each one and how they were “sorry to hear about little Kate”. The peanut butter she ate felt like it was stuck in her throat as she swallowed hard, tears blurring her vision as she came to realize that she had done it.

    It was official. Kate Bishop was dead.

    alone Chapter Summary Kate just wanted her mom.

    There was a car waiting for Kate early Thursday morning just as Kingpin said there would be. Kate had been sleeping when someone knocked on the door and they merely had to hold up a familiar- looking business card and tell her to get dressed for Kate to sleepily tug on clothes better fitted for fighting.

    “Shoes off,” Calvin ordered her, waiving a dismissive hand to the man who led her in. As Kate toed off her shoes once again, Calvin spoke. “You are dressed properly this time, at least.”

He would perform the same trick over and over, luring Kate into a false sense of rhythm before purposefully getting in the way of her hands. Kate was getting frustrated and upset each time he staggered back and rubbed his sore jaw.

    “Stop it!” Kate finally told him, dropping her hands. “I don’t want to hurt you!”

    “Nobody wants to be hurt, little birdie,” Calvin replied, cradling his sore jaw for a moment. “It is what must be done.”

    Kate had barely been taking lessons with Calvin for three weeks when he performed a move that made her lock up.

    Calvin had been pretty hands-off in terms of teaching Kate, preferring to run her through drills or have her be the one doing the fighting. But when Kate was punching at him and trying to determine when he would stop moving, she hadn’t been expecting the hand grabbing her wrist to yank her forward or the arm suddenly around her throat pinning her body to his.

    Kate was suddenly fourteen and in Central Park, walking home late at night when a man approached behind her. The panic hit her, filling her with fear as she squirmed and struggled.

    “Wherever you are, you are not there,” a voice suddenly said into her ear, soft and calm. “Fear is a helpful tool to help us survive but if we let it overtake us then it makes us stupid. You can be afraid, little bird, but do not let it show. Breathe.”

    Kate was then sixteen again, standing in a dojo with her teacher behind her, his arm still wrapped loosely around her throat as she tried to squirm and breathe.

    “Relax. I am not going to harm you. Take a moment to breathe,” Calvin instructed firmly, his voice still quiet and patient. “Just like when the wind was knocked out of you.”

    Kate had the phantom taste of blood in her mouth, an urge to sink her teeth into Calvin’s arm to get him to let her go, but part of her was locked in fear and she was struggling to escape.

    A hand fell onto her stomach and every muscle in Kate tensed up as she suddenly forgot how to breathe, tears prickling her eyes as she dug her fingers into the arm around her neck.

    “My hand will not wander, little bird. I want you to make it rise and fall with your breathing, deep into your belly, in through your nose, and out through your mouth,” Calvin instructed.

    “Take it off,” Kate managed to get out the strangled words, his hand uncomfortably hot against her sweaty shirt.

    “I will remove it when you breathe,” Calvin said firmly. “I have all day, little bird.”

    Kate doesn’t know how long she spent there trying to remember how to breathe. She closed her eyes and tipped her head back, wincing when it rested against a muscled shoulder before she focused on trying to make the hand on her stomach rise and fall. The hand finally disappeared and Kate was suddenly aware of the position she was in again.

    “You know how to get out of this.” Calvin stated the sentence with such certainty that Kate wondered if Kingpin told him of the classes she took. “So think, plan, and act.”

    “You are dismissed for the day,” Calvin told her. Kate doesn’t hesitate to dart out of the room, scooping up her shoes and not even bothering to put them on until she was safely tucked away in the car.

Frustration and distress flooded her and before she realized what she was doing she raised her own arm up to her mouth and bit down before pulling her arm away. She rinsed her mouth out again even though she knew there was still no blood and looked down at the teeth indenting her arm.

Kate couldn’t be bothered to care that she was in her pajamas as she rubbed the sleep from her eyes and stepped aside to let him in.

    “Good. You have done well so far,” Kingpin’s voice softened out and Kate felt uneasy at the abrupt change in moods. “You are working off your debt nicely. Your mother would be proud.”

    His words pierce Kate’s heart. Part of her wanted to believe that her mom really would be proud of her for handling everything. The other part of her told her that her mom wouldn’t like the decisions she’d made. Finally, a little sliver of her clung to the tiny bit of praise because she was so lonely and starved for any sort of positive contact after declaring herself dead.

    “He needs to die, Kate. Get justice for Mary. Avenge the girl who never got to live,” Kingpin ushered her even closer despite how Kate froze. “You can do it.”

    Kate wondered if Mary was scared when she died. She wondered if the man in front of her was scared. He was starting to rouse, his breathing increased as he slowly rolled his head around to try and shake himself awake.

    The man jerked his head up suddenly and inhaled sharply as his eyes flickered around the room before settling on Kate and narrowing.

    “Your time has come, Jacob,” Kingpin told him as he pushed Kate forward. “You will pay for your crimes.”

    Kate looked up at Kingpin helplessly with wide eyes, unsure of what he wanted her to do.

    “It’s up to you,” Kingpin reassured her and Kate stared at the man in front of her, suddenly aware of the heavy weight of the knife in her hand. “Go on.”

She pressed the tip of the knife against his collar but barely drew a bead of blood before she could go no further and immediately stumbled backward, dropping the weapon as her stomach churned.

    Kingpin picked the knife up and pressed it into her hands again. “I am not playing around, Kate. Do not test my patience,” he warned before he stepped back and gave her another chance.

Her hands were shaking, she belatedly realized it when she attempted to find a good length. Even if her mom was convinced she was going through a rebellious phase, Kate never had the urge to cut her hair short. She cried until her throat was hoarse and her mouth was dry, her tongue sticking to the roof of her mouth and the bitter taste of mucus lingering on her tastebuds. She wouldn’t be able to get the blood out of her shirt. She wasn’t sure how long she lay there for but she was jerked back to reality when there was a knock on the door. She belatedly realized that the sun was shining brightly through the window and her limbs ached when she unfolded herself.

    “Take a seat. Focus on your breathing. I will return,” Calvin instructed and Kate doesn’t protest, folding easily as she sat on the ground and tried her best to focus on the deep breathing Calvin had taught her.

    Calvin set the container down and reached out to pick up a thermos, screwing the cap off and holding it out. “Drink then.”

    Kate carefully took it and could smell that whatever was inside wasn’t water. It smelled floral and spicy but her mouth was dry enough that she took a sip. Something warm and bitter flooded her tastebuds. She thought it was some type of tea but she was too busy draining the thermos to try and regain moisture in her mouth that she doesn’t ask.

    Calvin said nothing and it was only when Kate had quickly finished the thermos does she realize what she had done and she embarrassingly held out the empty container. Her stomach felt uncomfortably full from just the liquid alone.

    “We will let that settle for a little while,” Calvin said and Kate could feel him eyeing her. “Would you permit me the opportunity to straighten out your new haircut?”

    Kate had forgotten for a few moments about it, reaching up to touch her fingers to her hair as she recalled how it had been sawed off with a knife. “Okay…”

    She resisted the urge to tense up when Calvin knelt behind her with a pair of scissors but she couldn’t help the flinch each time his fingers combed through her hair as she expected him to tug. He went about it methodically and Kate couldn’t help but wonder if he had done something like it before.

    She wondered if Calvin knew what she did. She wondered if Kingpin told him that she was going to do more than simply hurt people.

    “Sifu?” Kate whispered over the sound of snipping as little hairs fluttered to the ground. Calvin let out a low hum to show he was listening and Kate looked down at her fingers and flexed them, half expecting to still feel something sticky on her skin as she thought about how to string her words together. “I killed someone.”

    Calvin paused and Kate’s heart lept into her throat as the realization of what she had done crashed into her.

    “I killed him,” Kate repeated, her voice sounding flat to her own ears. “He was tied up and he couldn’t fight back but I still killed him.”

    Calvin set a heavy hand on her shoulder and Kate forced herself to take a deep breath when he gently squeezed.

    “He raped that girl. Mary. She was my age. He kidnapped her and raped her and killed her so I killed him.” Kate couldn’t stop talking, the words were flooding out of her mouth like a fountain. “His blood went everywhere. There was so much, I didn’t know that bodies could hold that much. It just wouldn’t stop. It came out and out and it was warm…”

    Calvin continued to cut and Kate swallowed hard, focusing on the bitter aftertaste of tea in her mouth.

    “He was a bad person and he did bad things but I feel so guilty that it hurts. If he is a bad person because he killed someone then I must be a bad person. Murder is illegal. It’s bad. I’m bad.” She heard the tink of the scissors being set on the ground as her hair was ruffled to knock any stray pieces of hair sticking to it off. “I could get arrested. I could go to jail. I did something bad and that makes me bad.”

    Calvin moved to sit in front of her once again, looking her in her eyes as he spoke. “Good people can do bad things just as bad people can do good things,” he said patiently. “If you do something that you don’t want to do because you must then that does not make you a bad person.”

    Kate felt her lower lip wobble and wished that she didn’t know how to cry. She was so tired of crying. “I could have refused.”

    “You are a smart girl, little birdie,” Calvin’s low voice didn’t hold the same mocking tone that Kingpin’s did sometimes. “You did what you had to in order to survive.”

    “I don’t want to survive,” Kate reached one of her hands up to dig her knuckles into her eye to try and stave off the tears that welled up. “I want my mom.”

    Through blurry vision, she could sweat that Calvin almost looked sad but a few blinks to clear her vision and the look was gone.

    “In life, there are things that we want and things that we need.” Calvin pulled the container of noodles in front of him once again. “You want your mother. But you need to survive.” He held the container out. “If you cannot find it to survive for yourself then survive for your mother. Eat.”

    Kate swallowed hard and thought about her mom scolding her for skipping meals. Even though she felt like she swallowed a rock that settled heavily in her stomach, she took the container and the fork and started to eat, trying not to think about it too hard.

    When their lesson eventually ended, Kate hadn’t thrown a single punch. She hadn’t even gotten up from the ground. After ensuring that she ate and evening her hair out, Calvin guided her through some breathing exercises.

    “I don’t want to hurt people anymore. I don’t want to kill again,” Kate confessed to him quietly before she had to leave.

    Calvin looked down at her, his hands clasped behind his back as he replied. “Wants versus needs, little bird.”

    Kate wished he had something better to say.

    Kate doesn’t want to remember that. She doesn’t want to think of the fact that someone like Hawkeye could have once been someone like Jonah or Calvin when they were kids. She wondered if Black Widow was like her once, just a kid in a shitty situation where she had to survive.

    It was the first time in a while anyone called her ‘kid’. She felt like her childhood was taken with everyone else in The Decimation.

    She went inside and showered before changing.     Eat some real food Kate. You know this isn’t for you -Mom

    The sob left her mouth before she could catch it. It was her mom’s handwriting, a little piece of her left behind.

    “I shot you!” Kate blurted out with wide eyes. “We need-- you need a doctor!”

    “I will go when your lesson is over,” he said firmly and Kate swallowed hard. “Blindfold on.”

    After their lesson, when the man’s shirt crisp white shirt was slowly bleeding into a soggy red, Kate sat in the back seat as he drove. He didn’t take her home, but rather to a place a few minutes away.

    “Kya, I need assistance,” he called out as he entered a store that was rather empty. The woman behind the counter with bronze skin and braids immediately jumped up and moved toward him. He was ushered further into the building and Kate was left standing by the counter, jumping when the woman told her to lock the door and turn the sign to closed.

    “The only other person here,” he hummed and Kate could feel Kya’s eyes on the side of her head.

    Kate’s eyes immediately snapped onto her, staring into firm blue eyes that held no remorse. “How old are you?” She demanded.

    Kate wasn’t sure what answer she was supposed to give. Should she lie? Stay quiet? Would Kingpin get angry at her for telling? She looked to the man for any sign of what answer she was supposed to give and he sighed.

    “Lay off her, Kya. She’s just a kid,” he murmured. Kya’s attention snapped back to him “She did what she was ordered.”

The smell was nauseating and Kate tried to look away but Kya wouldn’t let her. “Watch me work. You don’t get to look away from what you caused. You might find yourself in my shoes one day.”

    Kate swallowed hard as she forced herself to watch as the arrow was removed and the man was stitched up. When it was finished, Kate stared down at her bloodied fingers as her stomach roiled in protest. Her shirt was grabbed and she was yanked over to the nearest trashcan where she proceeded to empty her stomach.

    Kate looked up at Kya, a woman who appeared to be in her forties with strands of gray peppering her dark black hair. “My parents owed a debt. They’re… gone. It’s my debt now.”

    “The sins of the father are to be laid upon the children,” Kya mumbled, something Kate wasn’t sure if she was supposed to hear. Kya shook her head and straightened up. “Let’s wash our hands.”

    “Nuh, uh, don’t you go making a mess,” Kya scolded as she gripped Kate’s wrist to tug her into the back where there was a dinky old sink with poor water pressure. Kya wasn’t gentle but she was efficient. “You should clip your nails. Easier to get blood out from under them.”

    “Here.” Kya reappeared, startling Kate. Kate glanced over to her and saw that Kya was holding out a little metal tin. “Peppermints. They’ll help your nausea.”

    “Thank you,” Kate whispered as she opened the tin to look at the tiny candies before hesitantly taking one to place in her mouth. “Where’s--” She suddenly realized that she hadn’t seen the man in a while.

    “He’s still there,” Kya reassured her when panic must have crossed her face.

    When Kya finally deemed him safe enough to go, she stopped Kate right before she left and pressed a crumpled napkin into her hand. You hear me?”

    The next month passed by quietly until Kate was disturbed in the middle of the night by Kingpin. He hadn’t let her get changed out of her pajamas or even let her get her shoes as he tugged her outside and into a car. Kate glanced at the driver, recognizing him as the man who she had shot while blindfolded, before she looked down at her bare feet and tried not to shiver in the cold night air.

She hadn’t expected something to be pushed into her mouth right before he sealed it and Kate nearly choked on it, confused beyond anything. Calvin gave no hint that he had done anything as he secured a blindfold over her eyes.

The candy in her mouth had lost its hard shell and all that was left was a very sweet and tangy powder. It was so overwhelming that Kate nearly missed the metallic smell in the air and her stomach turned.

    Kate hadn’t planned on sitting at a bar at three in the morning in her pajamas after having killed her second man but there she was, perched on a stool next to Kingpin as the bartender mixed her a drink without questioning her age.

Kingpin laughed at her reaction and a new glass was pushed in front of her.

She settled on drinking anything set in front of her until her chest started to get warm and the room took on a fuzzy tint. It was almost like when she would stay awake to try and catch Santa as a child, sleep tugging at her so hard that it made it hard to keep her eyes open.

    “I think she’s had enough,” the bartender said at one point.

    “I’ll say when it’s enough,” Kingpin replied and gestured for another drink.

Kate lifted the glass to her lips without a second thought and swallowed it as fast as she could, coughing on it when she finished and wiped her mouth on the back of her pajama shirt. Kingpin chuckled, finishing his own drink before he gestured for her to stand.

Each step felt like trying to walk on jello that was rotating constantly. Kingpin kept a grip on her arm that kept her standing but after so many stumbles she squeaked when her feet left the floor entirely.

    “We’re home, Miss Bishop,” he told her and Kate tried to rub at her eyes to make the world stop spinning. “I’m going to help you inside.”

    Kate doesn’t care that she felt like a sack of potatoes when she was practically carried inside and settled on the couch. He grabbed the trashcan from the kitchen and set it at her side before ensuring she was laying on her side and then left.

    Kate sank into the darkness, letting the fuzzy feeling envelop her.

    Kate hadn’t really understood what people meant when they said they were dying because of a hangover. It seemed foolish, at the time, that a drink could cause so much pain.

    Kate’s head ached so fiercely that she could hear her heart pounding in her skull like a drum, ready to burst out at any moment. She wanted to sleep forever there in a cocoon on the couch even when she was sore and nauseous.

    Jonah came to get her for lessons and it took Kate a few moments to realize that he wasn’t a hallucination standing in her house. She wasn’t sure if she wanted to know how he got in as he hoisted her to her feet and out the door. Kate does not protest that she was in her pajamas from the night before and smelled like a liquor cabinet nor does she mention her lack of shoes. She curled up in the back seat of the car and dozed once again until they arrived at her lesson.

    She was too tired to deal with Calvin and his games. He had been so cold and distant the previous night and Kate still couldn’t believe he had taped her mouth shut in front of everyone.

    “Thank you, I’ve got it from here.”

    Kate hadn’t even realized they were inside until Jonah was settling her on the ground and Calvin was in front of her.

    “I killed someone again last night,” Kate blurted out before he could speak, reaching up to rub at her eyes. “And I’m really hungover.”

    “I can see,” he sighed out and briefly rested a hand on the top of her head before he pulled her hands away. Kate wanted to protest when he started to tie a blindfold around her eyes but he merely shushed her. “It’s for the light sensitivity, not training.”

    Calvin made her sit and practice breathing exercises while he went to go do… Kate wasn’t sure where he disappeared but she knew he would return soon.

    “Here.” Calvin’s reappearance startled her. “Hold out your hands.”

    Kate held out her hands and they were guided around a bottle which Calvin then prompted her to drink.

    It tasted like fruit juice of some kind and it was cold and refreshing. Kate hadn’t realized how thirsty she was until she was tipping the bottle back to drink more and more.

    “Careful,” Calvin tugged the bottle from her hands and Kate opened her mouth to protest. “You’ll make yourself sick like that. Here. Hold out your hand, I’m going to give you things to eat.”

    Despite her hesitation, it turned out the food was just oyster crackers.

    “Can I ask you about last night?” Kate started between bites as another cracker was set into the palm of her outstretched hand.

    Calvin let out a hum as she ate another cracker. “What about it?”

    “Why did you give me that candy?” Kate asked after taking a long sip from the bottle. “I don’t understand.”

    Calvin kept quiet for five more oyster crackers before he spoke. “Did it help?” Kate blinked at him so he repeated himself. “Did it help you?”

    Kate swallowed hard as she thought of the way the taste helped her stop panicking over everything that was happening. “Yeah.”

    “Then it worked,” he said matter-of-factly and that was that. Kate was quiet as he fed her oyster crackers and juice until her stomach was uncomfortably full.

    “Sifu?” Kate eventually broke the silence with a question that lingered in the back of her mind. “Do you think he’ll kill me?”

    Kate didn’t want to die and she was so afraid that she was going to make the wrong move one day and end up being the one tied to a chair to die.

    “You are too valuable,” Calvin replied gently, his tone lowering. “But remember this, little birdie. There are fates worse than death.”

    “Do you think I’m ever going to be free?” Kate whispered as she plucked at her pajama pants. Calvin’s silence told her everything. “I want to be free.”

    Calvin sighed. “Wants versus needs, little bird.”

    Sometimes Kate wondered whose side he was really on.

    A bottle of fruit juice being pushed into her hands made her hope that it was her. Calvin had done nothing but help her, even if Kate didn’t always understand it at the time.

    “Wants versus needs,” Kate repeated quietly before she drained the bottle of juice.

    They say that if you do something enough times then it gets easier and pretty soon it’ll be like riding a bike.

    Kate wondered if the person who said that ever had to kill anybody. She wondered if they had ever been through trauma enough times that a familiar numbness was starting to creep through them like a limb that fell asleep. She wondered if they ever feared that doing something enough times would make it get easier, if they ever waited in anticipation for the day they realized that it was indeed easy but it shouldn’t be.

    Kate had to learn to hurt people. It scared her that it was slowly getting easier to do so. Calvin taught her to break ribs and puncture lungs, Kingpin taught her to shoot a target without looking, and Kate taught herself to not react. That part wasn’t easy, not when she couldn’t apologize, try to help, or even try to convince them that she never wanted to hurt them in the first place.

    “Ah, my dear Kate!” Kingpin greeted her with a wide grin as she shuffled inside warily. Calvin’s face was blank and he barely acknowledged Kate other than with a nod. “I want to introduce you to a few people who will join you today, and occasionally in the future.”

    Kate watched as he set a hand on the first teenager, a girl a smidge taller than her with a stoic look about her. “This is Maya. She is very important to me.” There was an unspoken warning in Kingpin’s tone telling Kate that she was not to mess with Maya.

    He then turned to the boy next to Maya, who looked more laid-back and bored. “And this is Kazi.” There was no warning there which told Kate that Kazi was there merely as a formality, for what she has yet to know.

Kate wasn’t sure why she was being shown the sign of respect but Maya’s hand on her forearm gave a small squeeze before she let go. Kate slowly moved away and left the older teenagers behind as she collected her things.

    “You must be careful around her,” Jonah told Kate when she was safely tucked away in the car, the AC already turned on.

    “Who?” Kate glanced at him. “Maya?”

    Jonah nodded warily. “She is Kingpin’s honorary niece,” he told her and Kate’s blood turned to ice. “She’s the daughter of the head of the Tracksuit Mafia as well. She’s been training in mixed martial arts since could read. She is a dangerous person to get tangled up with.”

    Kate shrugged slightly, trying not to let his words scare her so much. “She’s also just a kid. It wasn’t easy being the heir to Bishop Security so I can’t imagine that being the daughter of the leader of the Tracksuit Mafia is easy either.”

    Jonah glanced at her in the rearview mirror. “Do not underestimate people you don’t know, Miss Bishop.”

    Kate thought of Calvin telling her to be grateful that Maya showed her mercy. Maya really didn’t seem that scary to her, even if she was totally going to kick Kate’s ass.

    Kate knew a thing or two about being judged for who you were related to. She hadn’t been allowed to be around other kids her age since The Decimation. She liked Kazi and Maya.

    “I’ll be careful,” she promised Jonah merely to give him a little bit of relief. Jonah relaxed slightly in his seat and Kate peered out the window.

    She hoped Maya and Kazi would join her again.

    Kate hadn’t expected Kingpin to show up only weeks later in the evening just before the sun was starting to set. Kate had been practicing making soup with the fresh groceries sent to her each week and she opted to just leave it to get stale rather than finish it up while Kingpin waited as he suggested.

    It wasn’t like she wanted soup anyway, she was just bored.

    She kinda wished that she hadn’t wanted for something interesting to happen. When she groaned about being bored to the empty kitchen, she meant that she wanted something interesting to happen like accidentally making her pot of water boil over or discovering that her box of pasta had one piece that was different from the rest.

    She almost wished that she could go back to being bored and making soup, she would much prefer it to whatever Kingpin had planned for her.

    For the first time, Kingpin glanced her over when she got changed to inspect her outfit and told her that they would have to update her wardrobe. Kate had plenty of outfits in her closet but she wasn’t about to argue that or show Kingpin her clothes.

    She followed Kingpin out to the car and nervously noted that there was a bow and quiver of arrows in the car. It was much too late to be doing archery although she supposed that never mattered to Kingpin.

    She wasn’t familiar with the part of the city he took her to, confused but knowing better than to question him. She followed him out of the car, aware of the way the driver collected the items from the backseat as she followed Kingpin inside the building.

    The building was rather full and lively, almost all of the people were men and they were all wearing some variation of the same tracksuit.

    A man stepped forward and Kate immediately clocked Maya standing at his side, Kazi lingering nearby with a few other men.

    The Tracksuit Mafia, Kate belatedly realized as Kingpin stepped forward to reach out and shake the man’s hand.

    “William,” Kingpin greeted him warmly, a smile crossing his face before he turned to Maya. “And Maya. It is good to see you.”

    Kate was glancing around the room and counting heads when a hand suddenly fell on her shoulder and she was gently pushed forward to be presented to William. “This is my newest muse, Kate.”

    Kate wasn’t sure how she was supposed to greet him so she settled on a brief smile and nod. William looked her over, a small frown crossing his face before it was gone and he was politely smiling back up at Kingpin. “I am anticipating to see what you wished to show me.”

    With Kingpin talking, Kate glanced around the room once more and caught Kazi’s eye. The boy grinned at her, giving a small waggle of the eyebrows before schooling his face. Kate couldn’t help but mirror him.

    “Mind yourself,” Kingpin’s hand suddenly squeezed her shoulder and Kate’s attention snapped back to him. “Do not make me have to tape your mouth.”

    “Sorry, sir,” Kate said quietly, her focus entirely on Kingpin. She let herself be led further into the building toward where a small gym was located. Men crowded behind her as Kate was led toward the center of the room. The lights suddenly felt hot and bright as she realized she and Kingpin were the center of attention.

    The blindfold was produced from Kingpin’s pocket and Kate doesn’t make a sound as it was tied around her eyes tightly, engulfing the room in black. She knew what was expected of her and held her hand out to let the bow be pressed into her palm. An arrow was then pressed into the other and Kate could hear Kingpin talking.

    “And now we let the magic happen,” he sounded enthused and Kate suddenly found his hand forcing her to spin in circles like she was a child at a birthday party getting ready to hit a pinata. She was dizzy and did her best not to stumble as the hands suddenly disappeared. Kingpin ordered the room to be silent and the sudden quiet was deafening.

    Kate listened hard for whatever direction she was supposed to shoot in, fearing that she would shoot the wrong way and hurt somebody.

    Then came a whistle and Kate jerked toward it, readying her bow. Part of her was grateful that Calvin made her spend hours blindfolded and listening for the softest of sounds. The whistle came again a few seconds later and she pulled her bow up to aim, squeezing her eyes shut despite the blindfold as she strained her ears to listen.

    On the third whistle, Kate took a deep breath before she let the string go and listened to the arrow whiz across the room and land with a thwack. She doesn’t dare remove the blindfold but the sound of sudden cheering screams in her ears told her that she had hit whatever target they wanted her to hit.

    The blindfold was suddenly tugged from her eyes and a large hand clasped the back of her neck fondly. Kingpin showed her off like a shiny new toy and Kate used whatever etiquette she had drilled in her from years of going to galas and fancy events to keep from breaking down right there.

    She hated the attention. She hated being shown off. She hated Kingpin and his dark grin and the way his hand clasped the back of her neck in a sign of possession that made her stupid body also feel reassured.

    “That was truly brilliant,” William complimented her and Kate politely thanked him with a fake smile plastered to her face. William then turned to Kingpin. “I think you are right. The Tracksuit Mafia will gladly assist in whatever she needs.”

    Kate felt like an object as she stood still, vaguely aware of the beads of sweat on the back of her neck making the collar of her shirt damp and stick to her skin uncomfortably. She scanned the room once again, her eyes meeting with the dark stare of the older girl standing next to William. Maya held eye contact with her although Kate couldn’t quite read what she was thinking.

    Kate wished she could take back her thoughts of wishing to see Maya and Kazi again.
