    interl. eleanor Chapter Summary Things had been strained between Eleanor and her husband even before his death. Things had been strained between Eleanor and her husband even before his death. He had a job he was secretive about, always guiding the conversation away from mentions of work and instead focusing on their daughter.

    Eleanor felt so foolish and blindsided when Wilson Fisk showed up to collect on a debt her husband owed, one that now fell to her head. She knew that it wouldn’t matter what she replied with, she would either willingly do what was needed or she would risk harm coming to her or her daughter. Neither of them could take another loss so Eleanor did what she had to do.

    Kate never really was the same after her father’s death. It seemed that the loss of Derek sparked something inside of her and she became obsessed with the need to get strong enough to protect herself and others.

    Eleanor was willing to indulge her at first, thinking it was her way of grieving and that enrolling in martial arts and archery lessons was a good outlet for her negative emotions. But it didn’t stop. Kate threw herself into the new activities with a fervor that Eleanor had never seen in her. As Kate rapidly started to climb the ranks, Eleanor thought about getting professional help.

    Kate had a meltdown over going to see the child therapist that Eleanor picked out, throwing herself on the ground and wailing for the first time since she was a toddler. Eleanor had felt so helpless, she didn’t know what to do. She couldn’t force Kate to do anything, not when her free time was already so small.

    Then came the first time that Kate used what she had learned and hurt another child at school. Kate was passionate about standing up for and protecting her friend that was getting bullied but Eleanor had to have a long conversation with her about the appropriate response to conflict.

    Kate really didn’t seem to understand. She kept interpreting Eleanor’s words as her telling her to step back and let things happen.

    “If you use what you’ve learned on someone who isn’t an active danger to you or someone else then I will drop you from all your classes.” Eleanor finally gave an ultimatum. That was the first big fight she had with Kate since Derek died.

    Kate screamed at her and stomped upstairs before slamming the door to her room shut.

    Eleanor knew she was hurting. She knew that she was grieving. She knew that her daughter needed patience and understanding.

    But she could only handle so much. Kate was twelve the first time Eleanor lost her cool and yelled back at her. Kate had been shocked silent, staring up at Eleanor like a stranger with wide eyes before she turned on her foot and went upstairs without another word.

    Guilt flooded through Eleanor and she approached her daughter later that night to apologize. She hadn’t realized how long it had been since she held her daughter until Kate was wrapped up in her arms as she cried.

    Despite her best efforts, it became a familiar cycle of Kate pushing Eleanor to her limit until she snapped back and then apologizing to her before spending the night curled up together. It was a carousel Eleanor could not get off of despite how hard she tried, going around in a circle over and over again.

    And then one day Kate came home late at night at the age of fourteen. Eleanor had been waiting for her, a familiar scolding on her tongue.

    She would never forget the look on Kate’s face when she arrived home.

    Eleanor’s little girl peered up at her with a blank stare, blood smeared around her mouth and her hair yanked loose from its ponytail. There were wrinkles in Kate’s shirt, a sign someone had grabbed and yanked the fabric.

    Eleanor’s heart dropped into her stomach and she didn’t think twice about stepping forward to swoop her daughter up into her arms. Kate was very tight-lipped about what happened but fell into Eleanor all the same.

    Eleanor pushed off all work that wasn’t urgent and stayed home with her daughter. Kate had violent nightmares that had her sleeping with Eleanor in her bed for a few weeks. Kate couldn’t be approached from behind and avoided Central Park like the plague.

    It got too much for Eleanor to bear. Even though she got Kate to agree to see a therapist about it, Eleanor saw the aftermath of the horrific attack and for the first time, she asked Wilson Fisk for a favor.

    Wilson Fisk seemed surprisingly sympathetic when Eleanor managed to get the story out. It wasn’t until Eleanor asked for the man who hurt her daughter to be dealt with that Wilson Fisk tilted his head back and chuckled.

    “You are aware of what you are asking, are you not, Eleanor?” He leaned forward in his seat.

    “I know.” Never would Eleanor have thought she was capable of the kind of hate that drove someone to kill. She could have never thought she would feel the bitterness that encompassed her heart and the wholehearted wish that the man who hurt her daughter dropped dead in the most painful and most violent way imaginable.

    “You aware that this doesn’t come free,” he continued on and Eleanor dipped her head down.

    “I do.” She knew she would rack up more debt but this was something she had to do. She needed to know her daughter was safe.

    Wilson Fisk grinned at her and held out his hand. It was like making a deal with the devil and she felt a little piece of her soul be chipped away for him to wear around his neck as a constant reminder that she owed him.

    “One more thing,” Eleanor tacked on before their meeting was dismissed. Wilson Fisk raised an eyebrow. “Make it hurt.”

    Wilson Fisk laughed like he knew that he had twisted and corrupted her to the point of no return. Some part of Eleanor was fine with that. It was what she must do. If she secured a future for Kate where she could be safe and happy then that was all that Eleanor needed.

    Three days later an envelope arrived to Eleanor with no return address. It was stuffed full of photographs, proof that Wilson Fisk had kept his word. It was bloody and violent and turned Eleanor’s stomach but some little part of her was relieved.

    A little teeny piece of her felt no remorse. She had killed a man, perhaps not with her own hand but the fault lay with her all the same.

    From there things went downhill. Wilson Fisk piled more work onto her to pay him back for the hit she asked for and Eleanor had very little time for anything else. She felt like a ghost in her own home, barely seen from her long hours working.

    Kate fought with her about never being home and Eleanor accepted the frustration because she too knew that it was unfair for her to be so absent. The fights only grew worse when her workload caused her to start missing important pieces of Kate’s life, her tournaments and competitions.

    Wilson Fisk knew that everything she did was for her daughter and he dangled that above her head. He’d never threatened to harm her, but perhaps it was worse that she knew he could. At every turn, each time she faltered or hesitated, Wilson Fisk would drag Kate’s name into the conversation to make her buckle.

    “Do it for little Katie,” he’d say and Eleanor knew that he could take everything she had worked so hard for away. Eleanor would never make her daughter worry about where she was going to sleep at night or wonder when her next meal would be.

    Eleanor continued to ride the increasingly worsening cycle that was her relationship with Kate: Kate acting out, Eleanor lecturing her, Kate yelling at her, Eleanor yelling back, Kate storming off in a huff, Eleanor checking in on her later, and then spending the night in tentative peace together where they were too tired to be angry.

    It had been just like any other day, Kate doing something foolish and Eleanor lecturing her when a sudden wave of nausea crashed into her and she wavered. She wasn’t sure what was happening at first as the room teetered side to side, Kate’s face twisted with frustration wavering into something unsure and cautious.

    Eleanor lost feeling in her feet, vaguely aware of the sound of people starting to wail and scream outside in the streets. Eleanor took a step toward her daughter when her toes felt cold, the feeling slowly creeping up her body.

    And then her feet were gone in a pile of dust and Eleanor didn’t even have time to hit the ground as her body rapidly disintegrated into nothing.

    There were so many things she had wanted to say at that moment, apologies for fighting, apologies for her absences, a reassurance that she loved her, a promise that she would be okay.

    Instead, she fell toward her daughter with her arms outstretched, wondering if her daughter was going to go too. Kate’s eyes were wide, just like the very first time Eleanor had ever raised her voice.

    Kate wasn’t going to go too. Eleanor knew that. She also knew what it would mean when she was gone.

    Her vision went fuzzy and she was enveloped into darkness, the last thought on her mind was hoping that Wilson Fisk would leave her daughter alone.

    The woman merely looked amused, quirking up one side of her bright red lips. Her inky black hair was pinned back neatly out of her face, a deep red cartwheel hat casting a shadow over her eyes. After a few moments of their stare-off, the woman lifted a hand and crooked a perfectly manicured finger to beckon Kate closer to her.

    Kate stiffened slightly, unsure of whether to leave Kingpin’s side and listen or stay put and ignore her. Kingpin glanced down at her and then she nearly stumbled when he gave her a firm push toward the woman.

    The talking didn’t cease as Kate slowly made her way around the edge of the room to stop next to the woman. Her heart was beating hard in her chest, unsure of how she was supposed to behave.

    The woman’s hand was gentle when she set it on her shoulder, gently hooking her fingers into the collar of her shirt and tugging her closer. “You know,” she started in a low tone, her voice a gentle purr. “It’s not very polite to stare, darling.”

    “I’m sorry,” Kate fumbled over her words, suddenly unable to look at the woman for fear of making things worse. She just kept thinking of the duct tape on her mouth and didn’t want to think about what Kingpin would do if she was staring.

    The woman let out a small hum, her fingers leaving Kate’s collar to instead tuck under Kate’s chin and lift her head. Kate knew what she wanted and forced her eyes to focus on the woman’s face.

    “What is your name?” The woman inquired. Kate’s eyes flickered to peer over at Kingpin but a finger tapped against her jaw. “Ah, ah, ah. Eyes on me.”

    Kate forced her gaze back onto the woman. “Blind-Shot,” she finally managed to get out, the name feeling odd on her tongue.

    The woman clicked her tongue against her teeth. “Manners, my dear. Shall we try that again? What is your name?”

    Kate had no idea what she had done wrong. She tried to piece together a correct response. “My name is Blind-Shot…” She was watching the woman for cues, seeing her still waiting for something. “What… is your name?”

    She was relieved when she got it right, the woman grinning down at her, flashing perfectly white teeth. “There we go,” the woman cooed. “You may call me Madame Red, my dear.”

    “What did I say about touching?” Kingpin’s voice suddenly broke the little bubble they were in and the hand dropped from Kate’s face.

    “Don’t be such a spoilsport, we were only introducing ourselves, weren’t we, darling?” Madame Red peered down at Kate. Kate glanced over at Kingpin nervously and slowly nodded her head. “You must let me borrow her some time.”

    “This one isn’t for loan,” Kingpin grunted out and Madame Red tutted in disappointment.

    “That’s a shame. I could teach this one some very nice tricks.” Her fingers snaked out to brush against Kate’s cheek in a caress. Kate wondered if all crime lords were sickeningly touchy or if Kingpin just had weird associates.

    “Perhaps another time,” Kingpin said before he pointed to the spot at his side and Kate immediately knew what he wanted. She pulled away from Madame Red and shuffled right back into the spot at his side, ducking her head down as his hand fell heavily on the back of her neck as expected. “It is time to go.”

    Kate let herself be led out of the room. It wasn’t until they were feet away from the car that the hand on her neck left.

    Kate hadn’t been expecting the feeling of something colliding with her face, her weight suddenly thrown backward at the force as she toppled into the gravel with the hit.

    “You do not, nor will you ever, embarrass me like that again,” Kingpin seethed down at her, looming over her in a way that made Kate’s stomach twist violently.

    “Yes, sir. I’m sorry,” Kate tried to get out, her cheek throbbing from the harsh hit. “I won’t do it again.”

    “No, you won’t,” Kingpin agreed in a slow tone. Kate braced herself when she saw him lift the cane in his hand and couldn’t help but cover her head when he swung. It collided harshly with her shoulder and she couldn’t stamp down the pained yelp at the impact. “I have been too lenient on you, Kate.”

    “I’m sorry,” Kate repeated, furiously trying to blink tears out of her eyes.

    “You will be,” Kingpin said. He pointed a finger to the right of her. “The road is that way. You will follow it.”

    Kate watched helplessly as Kingpin left her side and got into the car. Kate waited until the car had driven off to realize what he mean and slowly pushed herself up off of the gravel. She swiped a sleeve over her eyes before turning to where Kingpin had pointed and started to walk.

    She stared at her feet as she walked, trying not to get lost in her thoughts as she wondered how she could have done things better and had not angered Kingpin. She heard the soft sound of tire on pavement and tensed up as a car approached her. Hope briefly flickered through her that it was Kingpin but her hopes were dashed when she didn’t recognize the car.

    She picked up her pace, hunching her shoulders as she heard the sound of a window being rolled down.

    “What do you think you’re doing?”

    Kate froze at the sound of Madame Red’s voice, slowly turning to look at the vehicle to see the woman tucked in the backseat. “I--”

    “Come here,” Madame Red ordered and Kate hesitated before letting her feet take her to the door of the car where Madame Red was. The woman stared at her face for a few moments before tutting. “It’s miles from the nearest town, not a place for you to be wandering about in the dark. Get in.”

    Kate supposed that she really didn’t have any other option and opened the door to the car. She slid in next to Madame Red and tried to make herself small so that she didn’t brush against the woman next to her. The car started to drive again and Kate felt the knot of anxiety in her chest strangle her.

    “He’s such a brute of a man,” Madame Red commented and Kate flinched slightly when a hand touched her throbbing cheek. “Hold still, my dear.”

    Fingers prodded at her cheek and jaw, and Kate stiffened when a thumb brushed over her lower lip.

    “Open up,” Madame Red ordered and Kate could do nothing but obey, opening her mouth and letting Madame Red peer into it. “At least you didn’t crack your teeth.”

    When Kate was allowed to close her mouth, she swallowed hard a few times before speaking. “Thank you for the ride.”

    Madame Red let out a hum. “How old are you, Blind-Shot?”

    Kate froze at the question and the woman immediately laughed, high-pitched and airy.

    “There was no greater way of telling me you were young than that reaction right there,” Madame Red chuckled. “He’s never quite had them as young as you before. How long have you worked for him?”

    “Um,” Kate desperately hoped that Kingpin wouldn’t hear of the conversation. “Since The Decimation.”

    “Look at me when you speak,” the woman chided and Kate’s eyes flickered to look at the woman. “You are a very interesting person, Blind-Shot. I do hope that we get to know each other better in the future.”

    The car suddenly stopped and Kate spun her head around to look out the window. They were a few dozen yards away from the edge of the city.

    “This is your stop. Can’t have him catching you, after all,” Madame Red said. As Kate went to get out, the woman grabbed her wrist and tugged her closer before pressing something into her palm. “Stay safe, my dear.”

    Kate didn’t get a chance to look at the object until the car drove away. She peered down at the business card in a deep red, black font embossed on it with a single phone number. There was no name or other identifying features.

    Kate tucked it into her pocket and slowly headed into the city. She was trying to orient herself at the unfamiliar street signs, slowly walking down the sidewalk in a vain attempt of recognizing a landmark.

    “Hey!” Footsteps approached from behind her and Kate desperately wished that people would just leave her alone for the night so she could go home and decompress. “Hey! Bitch, I’m talking to you!”

    Kate picked up her pace, ignoring the man approaching her until a hand grabbed her injured shoulder and yanked hard, spinning her around.

    “I’m talking to you!” The man said, looking down at her with what looked like a drunken gaze. “Where do you think you’re going?”

    “Away from here,” Kate snapped at him, ripping her arm from his grip. His fingers immediately grab a fistful of her hair and yank hard, making Kate trip backward. “Let me go!”

    “This is your fault.” The cold metal of a knife against her cheek suddenly made Kate realize what was going on. “You just had to look at me. Why didn’t you look at me?”

    “C’mon,” Kate breathed out. “You don’t have to do this.”

    “I just wanted to talk. You ignored me, it’s your fault,” The blade felt cold on her flushed cheeks as he tugged her toward the nearest alley. The man continued to chant, “I just wanted to talk.”

    Kate suddenly felt like she was back at Central Park two years ago, a man yanking on her hair with ill intentions. Saliva pooled in her mouth as if her body was trying to beg her to sink her teeth into the man’s arm.

    The man pushed Kate’s stomach against the brick wall of the building, his hand pressing her face against the rough material, her throbbing cheek screaming in protest. “I just wanted to talk.”

    “Then talk,” Kate begged, squeezing her eyes shut as she listened to try and understand what the man was doing behind her. “I’m sorry for ignoring you.”

    The man behind her was muttering to himself. He was sick in the head, Kate could tell, and she knew that there wasn’t likely going to be a chance to negotiate with him.

    “I just wanted to talk,” he repeated before the knife disappeared from her cheek. Kate immediately elbowed the arm back and kicked her leg against his, a gurgled gasping escaping the man. Kate spun around and shoved at the body until she saw what she had done.

    Her hard shove with her arm had smacked the hand with the knife up and the man had a blade in his neck, choking on blood as he whimpered. Kate couldn’t move as he stumbled backward and fell, the knife lodging deeper with the harsh landing. She couldn't help but stare in horror at what she had done. She knew that if she got the man help then the police were going to get involved. Her name would go on record. She couldn’t have that. She was supposed to be dead.

    She crouched in front of the man, who eyed her in fear when a small whimper escaped his mouth, blood dribbling between her lips.

    “I’m sorry,” Kate breathed out, reaching a shaking hand out toward the knife. She slowly gripped the handle wet with blood and took a deep breath before giving one sharp shove and lodging it further. The man’s eyes rolled into the back of his head before he went limp, blood flowing down his neck and soaking his clothes.

    Kate pulled the knife out and stared at the chest that no longer rose and fell.

    “Oh fuck,” she breathed out as the realization of what she had done hit her. She bit back a wave of nausea, stumbling away from the man. She went to drop the knife before realizing that it had her fingerprints on it and forced herself to pocket it instead, shoving her bloodied hands deep into her pockets to hide them.

    She left the alley. She felt cold, and each step away from the alley felt like wading through molasses. She just kept walking down the street, agonizing over what she had done when she suddenly spotted a landmark she recognized.

    A sign for Fat Man Auto Repair was dimly illuminated by the street lamps and she found herself stumbling over to it. Kate reached a hand up to knock on the door before remembering that they were bloody and instead tapped her boot against the door instead.

    She felt anxiety twist her stomach harder with each second that the door went unanswered before it finally swung open and she was face to face with Maya. The scowl on the girl’s face immediately faded and was replaced with concern. Kate opened her mouth as if to explain but all that left her lips were apology after apology.

    Maya reached out to take her wrist gently and led her inside the building, the door closing behind her. Kate felt numb as she let Maya lead her further into the building. Kate suddenly found herself in the empty kitchen as Maya gently pressed on her shoulders to make her sit before holding her hand out to signal Kate to stay put and then disappeared.

    Kate looked down at her hands, the thought flickering across her mind that Maya had left behind fingerprints on her wrist from where she had touched the blood.

    Kate had killed somebody on purpose and nobody had even made her do it that time. The man was sick, he didn’t mean to scare Kate like that, but Kate had gone and killed him anyway.

    “Kate?”

    Kate’s head jerked up at the sound of William’s voice. The man frowned when he saw her, crossing the room closer to her and Kate suddenly wondered if he was upset that she had shown up. “I’m sorry!”

    William paused a few feet away from her. “Kate, what happened?”

    Kate could feel her composure crumbling quickly. “I’m sorry, I’m sorry, I didn’t mean to!”

    William closed the distance between them and Kate flinched when he reached for her. William dropped his hand, instead crouching in front of her to make her look at him. “Kazi told Maya to answer the door when he heard it. Maya came to get me when she saw it was you. What happened?”

    It was his gentle concern that finally made Kate crack and she burst into tears, reaching up to try and hide her face but William stopped her by gripping her wrists, preventing her from accidentally smearing blood on her face.

    “Shh,” William soothed gently, not at all upset that she was sobbing in her chair like a child. “You are safe here. I need you to tell me what happened.”

    Kate wasn’t sure where to start and tugged one hand free so that she could reach into her pocket and pull out the bloodied knife, holding it out to William with a shaking hand. She tried to stutter out an explanation but she could utter the words “I’m sorry”.

    William reached out and took the knife from her, not at all bothered by the blood starting to set deep into the grooves of the handle. “I need you to be honest with me, Kate. Did you do something to someone?”

    Kate could only nod jerkily as she tried to suck in a deep breath.

    “Are they still alive?” William then inquired and tears just fell faster as Kate cried harder. “Shh. I’m not upset. Look at me, Kate.”

    Kate forced her eyes to look at William. The man was the picture of calm, a small crease between his furrowed brow the only sign of worry.

    “I need to know where you left the body,” William told her. “And my men will handle the rest.”

    “K-kept walking s-straight,” Kate stumbled over her words, reaching a hand out to signal the direction she had come from. “In-- in the alley.”

    “Good,” William patted her knee. “I’ll be right back, okay? I’m going to send Maya and Kazi in here to help you clean up.”

    William was then gone in a blur and Kate stared down at her empty hands, realizing that William had taken the knife with him.

    “Hey. Kate.” Kazi’s figure suddenly swam into view and Kate peered up at him, reaching up to try and swipe away tears furiously with the sleeve of her shirt. “C’mon. Can you stand?”

    Kate shook her head, sure that if she were to stand then her knees would buckle.

    “Alright. Maya is gonna help you stand and then we’re gonna go to the bathroom to wash up.” Kazi’s tone was gentle. He scooted over to reveal Maya who reached out for Kate’s hands. Kate whimpered in pain when she was tugged upright, the throbbing in her shoulder renewing.

    She swayed on her feet, leaning into Maya before another figure braced her other side. “There we go,” Kazi hummed. “Easy now. Come on.”

    They made their way to the bathroom where they helped her stand in front of the sink. Kate felt like she wasn’t even in her own body as they twisted the water to hot and then rolled her sleeves up before guiding her hands under the water.

    She watched the water turn red and swirl down the drain before foamy pink soap bubbles joined it, only vaguely aware that Maya and Kazi were washing her hands. Kazi suddenly left her side and Kate flinched when something warm and wet touched her face, her eyes latching onto Maya’s dark eyes as before it fell to the washcloth the older girl was gripping. Maya gently wiped at her face with the warm damp cloth, cleaning away smears of blood and drying tracks of salt from heavy tears, her touch unusually gentle around the mark on her face.

    Kazi reappeared with a tank top, sweatpants, and socks in his grip, holding them up. “These are Maya’s. You can change into them.”

    Kate slowly reached out to take them when he passed them over. Kazi and Maya left her in the bathroom, closing the door behind them and Kate finally looked at herself in the mirror before looking away quickly, unable to even finish taking in the sight of her face.

    It was easier to take her shoes and pants off, changing into the soft sweatpants and socks easily. She struggled with her shirt, her shoulder screaming at her in pain as she tried to lift her arm up above her head. Taking off her shirt and bra was nothing but painful but Kate persevered and managed to wiggle her way into the tank top.

    She gathered her clothes up, slightly unsure of where to put them before she opened the door to the bathroom. She was surprised to see that Maya and Kazi were still there, standing outside the bathroom like they were guarding the door.

    “Here, I’ll take that,” Kazi reached for her clothes. “I’ll get them washed up and returned to you.”

    Kate did nothing to stop him from taking the clothes from her hands.

    “Damn,” Kazi suddenly let out a whistle and Kate looked up at him in confusion. “He got you good.”

    Kate followed his eyes down to her shoulder where she saw the beginning of deep purple bruising where Kingpin had hit her. “Oh…”

    Maya set a hand on her back and guided her back toward the kitchen and pushed her into the same chair before fetching two packs of frozen vegetables from the freezer, wrapping them in dish towels, and then approaching Kate. Kate held one to her face, the other against her shoulder as she sank back in the seat. Her stomach was still twisted in knots, worrying about what Kingpin was going to do when he heard what happened.

    She then thought about how he knew what happened to her in Central Park. He took care of the man who did it. Kingpin knew and he still made her walk home in the dark. A tiny piece of her wondered if he wanted to teach her a lesson, if he knew someone would try something.

    “You do not need to worry,” William appeared and took a seat at the table across from her. “The body has been taken care of.” His eyes fell onto where she was nursing the wounds she got. “Were those from him?”

    Kate slowly shook her head, her breath hitching again at the thought of what happened. “I made him angry. He took me to a meeting and I didn’t behave.”

    She watched William’s face shutter slightly. “Kingpin?” He asked quietly.

    Kate slowly nodded and William leaned forward. “Tell me what happened tonight, Kate.”

    Kate slowly managed to stumble her way through the story, telling him from the moment she left her house and met Elmer all the way to showing up on the doorstep of Fat Man Auto Repair.

    William listened with a furrowed brow, only interrupting her for clarification. He didn’t get upset with her at any point during her story and when she finished he merely inclined his head. “I see. Thank you for telling me.”

    “I’m sorry,” Kate repeated once again.

    “I know you are,” William soothed before he stood. “It’s too late out for you to head home. You may rest here tonight and someone will give you a ride in the morning.”

    Kate was too tired to ask if he was going to tell Kingpin. She nodded her head and followed him when he led her out of the kitchen. He took her to an office with a couch pushed up against the wall by a bookshelf and got her a pillow and blanket. Kate curled up on the couch, watching as William closed the door to the office and sat behind the desk to look over the papers there.

    She should have felt afraid being all alone with a man in the dark after the events of the night, but as her head hit the pillow and she slowly drifted off to sleep, she felt nothing but safe. infect Chapter Summary “You can do it,” Kingpin said, although Kate wasn’t sure whether the encouragement made her want to cry at the thought that she could or throw up at the thought that he looked like he truly believed it.

    Kingpin hadn’t lied when he told Kate that she would be sorry.

    He no longer gave her a little wiggle room. Any time that Kate stepped out of line, he would react swiftly and harshly. Kate learned quickly that she shouldn’t dodge him either, that just made things worse.

    If she apologized quickly and asked for forgiveness, then sometimes she could get away with him just grabbing her collar and shaking her a little. For what was perceived as more serious offenses, such as being late, not looking “presentable” enough, not remembering a detail that was told to her, or anything else Kingpin deemed wrong, he’d aim a hand and Kate would brace herself for a hit.

    She’d been hit before. It was an inevitability when doing martial arts coupled along with her penchant for getting into fights when she was younger before her mom threatened to stop her martial arts classes.

    But she’d never had a grown man hit her before, not with the force that Kingpin used. He was a big man, towering over Kate, his hand practically the size of her head. His hits would knock her back if she wasn’t paying attention, and she had to learn to snap her face away or duck her head down from wherever the hit was coming so he wouldn’t break her nose or split her lip.

    William tried asking about the hot skin on her face a few times but Kingpin never hit hard enough to leave bruises so Kate would lie. She wasn’t sure why she did, there wasn’t any reason to. She hadn’t been ordered to keep quiet, nobody was stopping her, but William, despite being head of the Tracksuit Mafia, was a little gentler to Kate than he was with his other men, a fraction of a softness that Kate saw when he would interact with his daughter.

    Kate never ended up finding out how William handled the dead body, nor did she ask. William never brought it up again after she woke up on his couch and got a ride home from Kazi on the back of his bike.

    The next month seemed to fly by and Kate’s life was filled with training, being shown off as Blind- Shot, and going on runs with the Tracksuit Mafia. When she wasn’t learning to fight or train, she found solace in Maya and Kazi.

    Kate Bishop was officially dead on papers and it was too risky to show her face around town, especially when Kingpin ensured that Kate had everything she needed so she had no reason to go out. She was very lonely in her big home but having a few others around her age helped.

    When she realized that Maya and Kazi were going to let her stick around and didn’t mind her tagging along, Kate started to do some research on sign language, determined to talk to Maya directly rather than waiting for Kazi to translate or for Maya to determine whether or not what she wanted to say was worth grabbing Kate’s hand to trace letters onto.

    Things had been going so smoothly that Kate hadn’t expected Kingpin to pull her aside one night and hand her a photograph.

    Kate had seen movies about spies and assassinations, but she stared up at Kingpin wondering if he was seriously asking her to find someone and then kill them on her own.

    “You can do it,” Kingpin said, although Kate wasn’t sure whether the encouragement made her want to cry at the thought that she could or throw up at the thought that he looked like he truly believed it.

    “I haven’t killed anybody by myself before,” Kate protested, squishing the memory of the man in the alley to the back of her mind.

    “There’s a first time for everything.” Kingpin wasn’t going to budge, he wasn’t going to give her extra help, and he wasn’t going to tell her what to do. “You have three days.”

    Kate was so sure that she was going to fail. She clutched the photograph in her hands, a name given to the face of the man, and she went home.

    It was difficult for Kate to track him down until she thought of using Bishop Security to do so. She found his job location and she found his home address.

    Kate dressed up the next day, glad that it was cold outside so she could wear a hat and scarf, and she set off into town to linger outside his place of work.

    She had never realized how busy the streets of New York had been until The Decimation had taken away half the world’s population. The streets seemed quiet, most of the abandoned cars on the road had been towed away and fresh paint had recently been applied over graffiti on the walls declaring that there was no hope.

    Kate paused in front of a recent spray-painted piece on a brick wall where a beautiful art piece of the Avengers once sat. The group now had their eyes blacked out, X’s over their mouths, and a large red circle with a line going through it. Kate stared up at the art, still able to barely see behind the spray paint that had started to drip before it set, leaving behind a thin coating.

    She stared at Hawkeye and her stomach twisted as she recalled that day when aliens invaded New York. The aliens came back but this time the Avengers didn’t stop them.

    People were angry. They were mourning. Kate pretended that suicide rates hadn’t shot through the roof or that shops selling caskets were making hundreds of thousands of dollars.

    Kate wondered if she was a bad person for not pretending to bury her mom. She had seen some people manage to collect the ashes of their loved one to keep in an urn, others choosing to bury empty caskets.

    Kate didn’t want to. It’s not that she couldn’t, she had more than enough money to do so, but she felt like doing it would make it real. It would just solidify the fact that Kate was on her own. She couldn’t even tell anyone who was pretend buried because her mom wasn’t supposed to be dead, Kate was.

    The sound of someone walking by behind her reached her ears and Kate waited a beat before turning her head to look at the man walking by. She huffed in irritation that it wasn’t him and glanced back at the paint.

    She stood there for what felt like an hour, waiting for the man from the picture. She finally heard someone behind her and turned, catching a glimpse of the man. She followed him from a distance, mapping out the route he took so that she would be prepared for the next day.

    She followed him the entire day, stuffing her hands into her pockets when her fingers got too cold and the sun started to set. She lingered outside his apartment for an hour after he entered before finally returning home to take a hot shower.

    The next day, Kate took the knife that Kingpin had given her long ago and with shaking hands tucked it into her pocket. She felt sick but knew that it was her or him, and she had to pick herself, she had to be selfish.

    Kate followed the man once again, waiting until it was dark out and he was walking home, grumbling to himself about his bad day at work. Kate finally tackled him into an empty alley, wincing when they both toppled to the ground.

    “What the hell?” The man demanded, trying to shove Kate off of him. “Get off me you hooligan!”

    Kate shoved his hands down, reaching to grab the knife in her pocket as he did his best to try and shove her off.

    “I’ll give you my wallet!” The man yelped when he saw the knife. “There’s no need for this.”

    Kate wanted to go fast but when she swung the knife, the man grabbed it with his bare hand in an attempt to stop her. Kate wrestled with him for control over the knife but he was a big man and a little stronger than her.

    The man managed to give the knife one quick shove and Kate bit down on her lower lip harshly to swallow down the cry of pain as the knife sliced through her shirt and grazed her side. She used her free hand to send a blow to his nose and when the man reached a hand up to try and protect his face Kate embedded the knife in his chest, unable to reach his neck.

    She pulled the knife out and slammed it down again, twisting it and listening to the man gasp in pain. Kate leaned her weight down on the knife and sat there until the man stopped moving, his hands falling limp beside him.

    Kate then saw the wedding ring on his finger.

    She took a shaky sigh and stood up, pulling her knife from his chest as she stared down at the man she just killed.

    “I’m sorry,” Kate whispered before straightening up. Her hand immediately flew to her side as pain burned through her and she remembered the knife hitting her shirt. Kate looked down and peeled her shirt up, doing her best to look at the wound under the dim street lights. “Oh fuck.”

    Kate pressed a hand over the wound and took a deep breath before she left the alley, pulling her coat tighter around herself to hide the growing patch of blood staining her shirt.

    The walk back home was agony, her breathing becoming labored as she tried to adjust to cause the least amount of pain possible when walking. Kate could feel the top of her pants get soaked with blood, breathing out a sigh of relief when she reached her home.

    Kate kicked off her shoes, flicking the lights on and immediately noticing the red staining her. It looked like someone spilled cranberry juice down her front.

    Seeing the blood made her feel nauseous. She slowly crept up her stairs and entered her bathroom, standing in front of the mirror and tugging her shirt up to reveal her stomach.

    Kate swallowed hard, her breathing shaky as she inspected the gash. It was about four inches long and while not too terribly deep, the skin was angry and red from the chafing of her shirt and pants. Tears prickled at her eyes when she tried to wipe away at the blood.

    When Kate was little, she cut her finger open on a knife trying to make herself a sandwich. It hadn’t seemed like a big cut but her mom saw it and immediately hook her to the hospital where the doctor put two stitches into it.

    But Kate could not go to the hospital, not when she was supposed to be dead and certainly not when she had just killed somebody. The police would undoubtedly be called if a girl showed up with a knife wound.

    So Kate rolled up a clean wash cloth and shoved it into her mouth to bite down on it and did her best to quickly clean the slice. She barely managed to clear away a majority of the blood to see the wound better when she got lightheaded and had to sit on the lip of the tub, breathing harshly as her hands shook.

    Kate finally managed to clumsily bandage it up, exhausted and shaking from the whole ordeal.

    She thought that perhaps she should feel worse that she had killed someone, but it was becoming frighteningly easy for her to just be numb. It was too exhausting to cry over every little death, to mourn the people she had to kill.

    Kate curled up in her bed and slept.

    She woke in the morning to her shirt sticking to her skin, blood crusting her sheets, and a fresh wet patch on her shirt from where she had bled through the bandages. Kate had to throw all her bloody laundry into the washing machine and rebandaged her stomach, not bothering to wear a shirt because she didn’t want to get another one bloody, instead just wearing a sports bra. Kate settled on the couch to watch television and ended up falling asleep once again.

    Kate kept taking naps throughout the day, unable to shake off the fatigue gripping her as she kept changing the bandages on her side. She was pleased that it had stopped bleeding so much but the skin was still an angry red that made her worried.

    Kingpin finally came to see her that night and commended her on the kill. When Kate tried to tell him about her injury and ask for a doctor, Kingpin told her that she should have been faster and that it was a learning experience for her.

    Kate knew better than to ask again. She simply nodded her head and listened to Kinpin talk.

    When the time for her next session with Calvin came, Kate hadn’t wanted to go. She felt miserable, like hot poker sticks were jabbing her abdomen with each breath, and the skin around her injury was warm to the touch.

    Kate did her best to look after the wound but she wasn’t a professional.

    She was taking a nap on the couch when Jonah came for her. The man frowned at her when she stumbled out toward the car and Kate ended up accidentally falling asleep once again in the backseat as he drove her.

    Jonah walked in with her and Calvin’s brow furrowed when he saw her. Kate swallowed down the nausea churning in her gut and quietly pleaded with Calvin to let her have the day off or give her something easy to do.

    “Maya and Kazi are joining us today,” Calvin replied, folding his hands behind his back. “You may rest until they arrive.”

    Nope.

    02-51-36-07

    Kate tried a few different combinations until finally the lock gave with a click and she was finally able to tug it open.

    The inside was different from what she remembered. There was no sign of keepsakes or jewelry. Instead, there was a large envelope, a black handgun, money in different currencies, and passports.

    Kate swallowed hard. Her fingers found the gun. There was no orange tip on the end to tell her that it was an airsoft or a BB gun like she used to shoot at summer camp. She handled it carefully, delicately setting it on the ground beside her before her fingers found the passports. There were more than two.

    She flipped the first one open. It was hers. She opened the next one. It was… also hers. But the name was wrong. ELIZA BARNETT was printed neatly where the name went and the month and day of her birthday had been switched around.

    In total, Kate had three passports for her, all with different names. Her mom had two. She set aside the stack of passports. She reached for the envelope when her eye caught on a small package that had been hidden behind the passports. A little purple box with a bow stuck on top of it. Kate reached for it. Her fingers hooked under the ledge and lifted the top off.

    It was a shooting glove, one of the expensive kinds that archers used to protect their fingers. Kate lifted it out of the box and already knew that it would snugly fit her hand like it was made for her. It probably was. Her mom probably knew she would snoop in the closet for presents so she tucked it away.

    Kate had nearly forgotten about the envelope left. It was somewhat heavy, thick with what Kate assumed were papers. She untucked the flap and tapped the contents out until she could pinch and pull the rest of the stack free.

    It was photographs. The first one was a familiar face and it had startled Kate so badly that she dropped the stack, jolting backward in fear and tripping over the gun. Pictures fluttered and scattered the ground.

    Kate could scarcely breathe. Her eyes flickered from picture to picture, taking in the carnage and violence displayed. Photos from all different angles, time-stamped over a course of hours, horrific and red.

    It had been dark the day Kate was assaulted but she knew that face. Kingpin said his name had been Ben Henderson. Kate hadn’t exactly believed him when he told her that her mom asked him for a favor. Maybe her mom did ask him to deal with the man who hurt her. But that left the question of why her mom had the pictures. Why were they there? What was so important that she had to keep the photographs detailing the torture and death of a man she condemned?

    Kate didn’t realize she had shoved her forearm into her mouth to bite down on it until the metallic taste flooded her mouth. It just made her think of that night. Kate stumbled to her feet, slipping on the stack of passports on her way out of her mom’s office all the way down the hall until she burst into the bathroom and poured water all down in front in a frantic attempt to swish her mouth out with the tap.

    She hunkered down in the bathroom, her heart pounding so hard against her chest that she couldn’t breathe, terrified as she was reminded of everything she had tried so hard to forget.

    She wanted to demand answers. She wanted to scream at her mom. She wanted to cry. She wanted a hug.

    In the end, she was only able to do one of those things, tears staining the sleeve of her shirt right next to the bloody bite mark.

    Kingpin came to collect her the next day. Kate had a rough night camped out on the floor of the bathroom, the shower cranked on as she listened to the sound of water falling against the tile, squeezing her eyes shut and pretending it was rain.

    Kingpin merely shoved an outfit into her arms when she answered the door. Kate went upstairs to go and change, confused when she saw it was formal wear, dress shirt and jacket and all. There was a tie. Kate couldn’t remember how to knot it. Perhaps it was foolish but after trying to watch YouTube videos and failing, Kate returned downstairs where Kingpin was waiting, tie in hand, and quietly asked him for help.

    Kingpin laughed as if she had asked him something silly. Yet he reached out, his large hands unusually gentle as he settled the tie around her neck and began to do it. She half expected him to fasten it so tight she choked but he seemed insistent that she was actually presentable.

    She wanted to ask him why he chose an all-black suit. Wanted to ask why not a dress. Wanted to ask why a black dress shirt too. But she didn’t, wisely keeping her mouth shut as he fixed her collar.

    “Keep your head down,” he instructed. “And your mouth shut.”

    “Yes, sir,” Kate mumbled. She didn’t need to be told twice, she learned her lesson long ago. “May I ask where we’re going?”

    “To a charity auction,” Kingpin replied. He then braced the back of her neck, the same hold that Kate loathed and craved at the same time. “Come.”

    Kate followed him obediently out to the car. When they arrived and entered, she hadn’t expected to see Madame Red there as well. The woman had no red on her. In fact, it dawned on Kate that everyone was dressed in a black-and-white ensemble.

    Madame Red clicked her tongue against her teeth when she saw Kate. Kate flinched slightly when a hand moved toward her head but all Madame Red did was attempt to tame the nest of unruly hair on the top of her head. It was at that awkward stage between short and long where she couldn’t pull it back out of her face but it fell in her eyes. All Madame Red was comb it back with her fingers, her false nails scratching lightly against Kate’s scalp.

    Kate pretended she didn’t see the other crime lords there. She let herself indulge in the fuss before she was ushered off up a staircase to the side. A large hallway overlooked the ballroom of the building they were in. Kate stood next to Kingpin and felt his hand settle on her shoulder, heavy but not tight and harsh.

    “You turn seventeen today,” Kingpin stated, his voice unusually quiet to keep others from overhearing. Kate had completely forgotten that it was her birthday. She suddenly felt sick. “It is a shame your mother is not here with us.”

    “Yeah.” Kate’s voice cracked slightly. She tried not to think about the gun or the passports or the pictures still scattered on the floor of her mom’s office.

    “Do you know what this event is?” Kingpin inquired. Kate stared over the sea of people donned in black formalwear.

    “No,” Kate whispered.

    “This is the Katherine Elizabeth Bishop charity auction,” Kingpin said. Kate felt like the air was knocked out of her lungs. “Poor Eleanor. Her first year without her daughter. Grief does terrible things to people.”

    “You took my suggestion,” she finally managed to croak out. She had been the one to suggest it long ago when Bishop Security stocks were tanking.

    Kingpin looked down at her, a smile across his face that didn’t look as malicious as she was used to. “You insisted upon it. We have a deal in place. You work for me and…”

    “You protect me,” Kate finished quietly. “And I get the final say about Bishop Security.”

    Kingpin chuckles, low and warm. “Atta girl,” he hummed, patting her shoulder firmly.

    “So the black formal wear,” Kate started unsurely, “is to represent… mourning?”

    Kingpin swept an arm out over the crowd, gesturing to the room of people. “They’re all here for you. Your memory.”

    Kate felt sick. She stared over the crowd of people and watched as the auction began. Pieces of artwork were being sold at absurd amounts with the promise of going to charity just so rich people who lived cushy lives could pat themselves on the back and feel better about themselves. Kate doubted much of the money would actually see charity. She wondered if the people knew that too.

    “Here.” A glace was delicately dangled in her eyesight and Kate peered up at Madame Red in surprise. She reached out to gently take the flute of champagne offered out to her. “Happy birthday, my dear.”

    Kate didn’t know what to say in response. Her mouth was dry and she slowly placed the rim of the cup against her lips and tipped it to take a swallow. She didn’t care very much if someone slipped something into it. She took a sip. And then another. And then she downed the whole thing and licked the foam off of her lip.

    When a new glass was pressed into her hands, she did not hesitate to drain that one. At one point between glasses, she had started to lean on the railing, someone’s hand ghosting over the back of her jacket as if to snatch her up if she tipped too far forward over the edge. She eventually rested her head on an arm lying on the railing and watched the art pieces being sold.

    More glasses. She got tipsy. Then drunk. She swayed on her feet, her fingers wrapped firmly around the railing as she blinked away spots in her vision. When she’s peeled away from the railing by large hands, she found herself blinking up at Kingpin, tired and sad.

    “A little too much, hmm?” he hummed. “Come.”

    Kate nearly tripped down the stairs descending them. Kingpin kept a firm grip on her upper arm to keep her steady as if she were a naughty child who had run off one too many times. She doesn’t recall Madame Red disappearing but when looking for her she came up empty. Kingpin secured her in the car.

    They pulled over once so Kate could puke in the grass next to the road. She emptied her stomach so violently that she got a nosebleed. The blood, the alcohol, and the reminder of her birthday was all too much and she found herself crying. She half expected Kingpin to get furious with her but he was almost gentle with the way he handled her. He pinched her nose with his handkerchief and wiped the blood away from her chin. There was a labeless bottle of water that she was offered to rinse her mouth out.

    Kate had thought she’d return home. Instead, she was surprised when they pulled up to Fat Man Auto repair. The men who usually greeted Kate when she showed up at odd hours of the night were silent when Kingpin loomed at her side.

    “I’ll get the boss,” one volunteered before fleeing and leaving his coworkers behind. Kate felt like a mess and really hoped that Kingpin didn’t have any other plans for her.

    “Kingpin.” The familiar voice of William washed over Kate and she found herself untensing slightly. “What brings you here at this hour?”

    “I’m afraid that somebody has had a little too much to drink tonight,” Kingpin chuckled, giving Kate a little shake. Kate felt nausea bubble in her gut. “I cannot leave her alone in good faith. I hoped you would provide her with shelter for the night.”

    “Of course,” William said without hesitation. Kate hadn’t even realized he moved until he was right in front of her, his hand replacing Kingpins.

    “Goodnight, Kate,” Kingpin told her.

    “Goodnight, sir,” Kate managed to get her mouth to form.

    William started to walk her inside as soon as Kingpin’s car disappeared around the corner. They ended up in the kitchen area and Kate did her best not to flinch when he undid the buttons on her jacket and gently slid it off.

    “Easy there,” William murmured as his fingers started to tug loose the tie. “How much have you had to drink?”

    Kate looked down at her hands and tried to count but the glasses blurred together and she ended up giving a small shrug. “Sorry,” she whispered.

    “Can I inquire the occasion for this amount of alcohol consumption?” William inquired as he set the jacket and tie on the table neatly folded, urging Kate to take a seat.

    Kate was quiet, watching as he moved toward the counters to rifle around in the cabinets. She rested her head on her arms on the table and her gaze followed him around the kitchen.

    She jumped slightly when the toaster popped. She finally managed, “It’s my birthday.”

    William stilled for just a moment, so quickly that Kate almost wasn’t sure if he stilled at all. “I see.”

    Kate dozed at the kitchen table until a plate of toast and a glass of water was set in front of her. William doesn’t have to give her an order to eat and drink.

    William had a long night of work ahead of him but still made up the couch in her office for her. Kate curled up to sleep under the blanket and barely heard him speak before she fell asleep.

    “Happy birthday, Kate. May the next year be kinder than the last.”

    With Dolly’s bedazzled flip phone in her grip, Kate dialed the number she memorized. She hadn’t really considered the fact that he might not pick up. She wondered if Kingpin was meeting up with the other crime lords and that was why Jonah was unavailable.

    “No answer?” Clementine asked softly when Kate punched in the number a third time. “Do you have someone else?”

    The one-year anniversary of The Decimation came. Kate didn’t even realize it at first. She didn’t spend a lot of time catching up with the media anymore. It was always depressing to hear about the latest murder-suicide because of someone who was lost.

    The school held a vigil. They took down the papers that had been zip-tied to the fence. There was a fire, and the principal would take down each paper one by one, holding it up for everyone to see the smiling face printed there, announce their name, and then toss it into the flames while everyone said their goodbyes.

    “I went with a sweet white,” Esmeralda interrupted once again. Kate spotted a bottle of wine in her hand and two glasses dangling from between her fingers. Kingpin grunted as she set them down and then popped the cork from the bottle.

    Kate eyed the glass as it was filled halfway and then pushed towards her. She knew she shouldn’t drink, nothing good ever came from it, but once again, she refused to be rude. So she reached out and took the glass and took a sip.

    “I miss my mom,” Kate suddenly said. All the talk about mothers made the grief press down on her all over again.

    “I know,” Kingpin said, his face softening slightly. “She was a competent woman and her only concern was protecting you.”

    Knowing the things she did now, Kate wished she had been kinder to her mother. They wouldn’t have fought so often. Kate could have had a better relationship, she could have said the things that were left unsaid, she could have told her mom that she loved her more.

    “I’m not Hawkeye,” Kate said quietly. “Hawkeye doesn’t…” she trailed off. Hawkeye was good. He helped people. He saved the world. If he met Kate he wouldn’t be on her side.

    “You’re not Hawkeye,” Kingpin agreed. Kate startled when he reached out and set a hand on hers. “You’re even better. You’re my little bird. And you’re going to do great things.”

    Kate had many new names. Blind-Shot. Archie. Tara. Fetch. Little Bird. It felt like she didn’t even know herself anymore. The person staring back at her in the mirror was a stranger.

    Kate was broken from her thoughts by a yawn. As embarrassing as it was, Kingpin merely seemed amused at it.

    Kate peered down at her empty glass, unable to recall when she finished it. The bottle was still half full which told Kate she hadn’t been drinking like a sailor.

    “Esmeralda!” Kingpin called out. The woman entered and Kingpin gestured to Kate. “Show her to where she’ll be sleeping.”

    Esmeralda nodded her head and turned toward Kate. Kate knew a dismissal when she saw one and stood, grabbing her bag and following Esmeralda down the hall.

    The bedroom was immaculately clean and orderly. Esmeralda told her there were fresh towels in the bathroom and it was stocked and that she could call for her if she needed anything. When the woman left, Kate immediately closed the door and twisted the lock on it, a small burst of relief flowing through her as she heard the click.

    She didn’t trust Kingpin, not exactly, but she was exhausted and she had to believe that the man wouldn’t enter her temporary bedroom. She shuffled toward the bed and grabbed the covers, toeing off her shoes as she slipped under them.

    Kate dreamed that night of mothers and the lengths they would go to protect their children She dreamed of her mom and the love in her eyes and the nights when everything was forgiven and it was just the two of them.

    Chapter Notes See the end of the chapter for notes Kate sat at the empty table the next morning, frozen in place and terrified to make a wrong move. Kingpin had business, that was what she was told, so it was just her to eat breakfast.

She felt like she was going to be sick but she knew that it would be rude to turn away food and she wasn’t sure what Kingpin’s plans for her were yet but she knew she wasn’t supposed to leave.

    Kate jumped when a plate was set in front of her. She hadn’t heard Esmeralda enter the room. She stared down at the plate of eggs, sausage, and toast, and swallowed down a bout of nausea.

    “What can I get you to drink?” Esmeralda inquired. Kate picked up a hint of an accent smothered under the formality.

    “Black.” Kate wanted nothing more than to make a sugary concoction from the coffee but the last time Kingpin gave it to her, he gave it to her black, so black it was.

    “Careful,” the woman warned softly. “It’s hot.”

    Kate wrapped her fingers around the cup and let the warmth seep into them, watching the skin grow red until she was forced to let go because of the sheer heat.

    Kate eventually decided to give up on proper table manners and propped her head up on her fist, elbow braced on the table as she pushed her food around the plate with her fork.

    “Is something wrong?” Esmeralda eventually questioned. “Can I get you something else to eat?”

    Kate straightened up immediately at the question. “No! I mean-- it’s okay. I’m just not really hungry right now. I don’t usually eat breakfast.”

    “Come,” William stepped around his desk. “Let us prepare.”

    While waiting for the others to gear up, already dressed herself, William pulled Kate aside.

    “A gift from Kingpin,” he said as he held out a box. Kate’s heart dropped into her stomach at the thought of what could be in the box. Still, she swallowed down her fear and reached out to lift the lid.

The mask was a deep purple, almost black, and Kate knew that Kingpin was aware her favorite color was purple. She couldn’t tell if he was mocking her or trying to take away yet another thing she still clutched close to her.

In front of her was a man to be feared, the father of the girl whom Jonah warned her to stay away from, a man ready to do anything to keep going.

    The first time Kate ever saw James Bond, she thought it would be funny if Hawkeye did similar things. Watching him dive off of a building to shoot at aliens made her think of grappling hooks and ziplines and all of the things spies did.

Her mom found her that night after she was supposed to be in bed, looping together belts and picking at her clothes in some attempt to make the costume better. Kate thought she’d be furious.

    “Explain what happened,” William told her firmly, his eyes set firmly on her. “Now.”

She told him about the man with the deep voice and the red mask. Admitted that she trained briefly with a professional in knives who taught her how to escape blades. Admitted the blood on her side was hers.

    “Here,” the man next to her said quietly and a wad of gauze was pressed against her palm. “Hold that there. We’ll get it looked at soon.”

Kate wasn’t sure what he did with the package but she stood at the end of the lineup of the men who went with him and waited. She wasn’t sure why they were waiting but she stood there with a hand pressed over her stomach and waited.

    William started at one end of the line. He spoke in a different language, asked his men a few questions, patted their shoulder or clasped their upper arm before dismissing them. One by one, down the line until Kate was the last one left and she was left looking up at him.

    “Kate,” he addressed her, his voice hoarse as if he had been shouting. Which he had been, and it unsettled her to hear the aftermath. “Show me.”

    Kate followed his eyes to her stomach and hesitantly peeled her hand away. Crimson dripped onto the floor in small beads as Kate peeled away the gauze and lifted her shirt to expose the wound.

    “A stab wound,” William said in a quiet tone. “He’s not one for knives. Doesn’t seem like the type to stab.”

    Kate does not volunteer what Tiago had taught her, what Madame Red had promised in the empty room with a spring-loaded blade. “Who was he?”

    William tilted his head to the side and regarded her for a moment. Finally, he said, “Daredevil.”

    “Daredevil,” Kate repeated his name. “Why was he there? I thought he ran in Hell’s Kitchen?”

    William’s lips pressed together unhappily. “Daredevil has an… unusual relationship with Kingpin. Some might call it an obsession. The burning desire for justice. He interferes with everything he can to get the upper hand on him.”

    “I fought Daredevil,” she stated aloud, more to herself than him. She wondered if the man looked at her and for a moment hesitated because of her age or if he just saw her as an extension of Kingpin, a problem to get out of the way. “He tried to get me to give up the package and walk away.”

    He had offered her an out. His fight wasn’t with her, it was with Kingpin and Kate was just tangled up in it all.

    “You will not see him again,” William stated, sure surety in his tone that Kate couldn’t help but believe him. “Do you have someone you trust to look at your wounds?”

    Kate stared up at him, nodded her head, and lied. She had the resources, but trust was hollow and missing. “I’ll be okay.”

    William seemed reluctant to let her leave but she knew he had business to take care of. “I’ll get Tomas to give you a ride.”

    Kate nodded, flinching slightly when his hand fell onto the back of her neck.

    “You did well,” William told her. Kate didn’t mention her mistakes or her slip-ups but eyed him. “The package was the only thing that mattered. You did well.”

    Kate wasn’t sure what the package was but she couldn’t help but preen slightly under the sincere warmth in his tone.

    “Get yourself looked at, take a shower, and go to sleep,” William instructed, gently squeezing the back of her neck before letting go. “You’ve earned it.”

    Madame Red followed through on her promise to get Kate medical care if she ever had to use the moves Tiago taught her.

    The doctor was a man and while he was cold and curt, he was nothing but professional, even when he asked Kate to take her shirt off. Kate distracted herself by thinking of what her mom would say if she caught Kate sitting on the very expensive dining room table in cargo pants and a sports bra as a stab wound was stitched up.

    The man left behind things for Kate to continue to care for her wounds, gave strict instructions on how to care for it and how to catch an infection before it got bad. He left his personal number for her to call if she needed help again before he was gone, leaving Kate behind in an empty home.

    Kate hadn’t failed to notice that Kingpin had repaired her window and cleared up the mess made by the intruder. There were fresh locks on the windows, recently installed, and Kate hated the bloom of gratefulness that settled in her chest as Kingpin followed through on his promise of protecting her.

    Kate climbed the stairs but had to take a break when she got to her bedroom, collapsing on the edge of her bed to take a few breaths. She scrunched her face up and then after a few moments realized she was still wearing her mask. She reached for her pocket where she had stored both the glue and the solvent, snagging the solvent and peeling the mask off of her face.

    She once told her class that she wanted to be Hawkeye when she grew up.

    Now she was the kind of person that vigilantes like Daredevil go after.

    Kate woke up the next morning and was shuffling through the house, trying to ignore the pain radiating through her damaged body.     Kate wasn’t sure why he gave it to her in such a way. A reward for her work the previous day? A taunt? A reassurance? A threat?

    It was a relief. Kate hated it but her shoulders relaxed and she stared down at the photograph. Kingpin hadn’t made her do it. Someone had threatened Kate and he took care of it just like he promised he would.

    Kate took the photograph with her to the kitchen.     She downed two dry and trudged over to the freezer to yank it open. While Kingpin always ensured the food he had delivered contained fresh fruits and vegetables, Kate also had an assortment of frozen vegetables at her disposal for the very purpose she was about to use it for. She grabbed a mixed bag of peas and carrots before pressing it to the bruising on her ribs, shuddering at the cold on her bare skin. She hadn’t changed out of the clothes from the previous night. Donned in black cargo pants and her sports bra, Kate felt like she needed a good shower. She also knew she had to be careful about the stitches on her side from the wound she received.

    There was a knocking at her door, loud and incessant. Kingpin usually only knocked a few times, firm and sharp. Kate didn’t have many visitors. She groaned and closed the freezer, shuffling her way toward the front door. She twisted the lock and swung it open, her face devoid of emotion.

    Maya stood there, hand still raised. She lowered it and raked her eyes over Kate, eyes darkening as she took in her appearance.

    “Hey,” Kate waved to get her attention before pressing the frozen bag of veggies to her side again. She hated making Maya read her lips but her brain was sludge and she couldn’t be bothered to sign. “Why are you here?”

Her stomach twisted. She moved back to watch the roof for a few seconds.
